# Unresolved risks and certification blockers

Originally generated 2026-09-23. Status refreshed 2026-10-05 against the current source and test evidence; historical observations are labeled as such.

## Critical

1. **No production certificate.** Current architecture documents correctly describe certification as gated. No live trading authority is inferred from code, configuration, or a passing test.
2. **Multiple composition roots and authorities.** `fusion.ts`, platform orchestration, and intelligence orchestration can represent competing state/execution paths. Establish one authority graph before any live enablement.
3. **Uncommitted sensitive-surface changes.** The working tree includes execution, signing, reconciliation, config, release, and UI changes. They require review and reproducible evidence before any merge or packaging decision.

8. **Paper account recovery remains unavailable.** Independent Astra review accepts the canonical v3 schema/migration, deterministic SYLPH-JCS-1 codec, isolated explicit-genesis bootstrap, and read-only journal, owner/fencing, and command-lifecycle consistency verifiers. Verified v0/v1/marker-v2 migration creates schema without account history; bootstrap can create only an explicit-genesis account in `BLOCKED` state with one `PENDING` generation and two epoch-0 events, after the caller holds the same-host startup OS lock. The recorded operator identity is not authenticated. The journal verifier now streams both account-sequence and generation-sequence axes inside one `BEGIN DEFERRED` snapshot, preflights genesis/event BLOB sizes before projecting bytes, and returns fixed-size diagnostic samples with exact counts/digests; it always remains held and admission-ineligible. Independent Astra accepted this bounded journal slice at source SHA-256 `6372752B0173B46A21F697188621E4981D1076B2488F5B08461157103C26D87E`; focused journal/owner/command tests pass 24/24. The owner verifier still `.all()`-materializes all account/event rows and retains history-sized structures; the command verifier still `.all()`-materializes events, commands, reservations, reports, and fills into maps. Therefore this is not an end-to-end memory bound and there is still no aggregate resource/cancellation budget. First-failure diagnostics can differ from the old materialized verifier when one history contains multiple simultaneous corruptions, while both reject the history and no clean result escapes. Verifiers always return held and admission-ineligible; fence/acquire row adjacency does not prove a shared transaction. No general event writer or full semantic event fold/projection rebuild, durable owner lease/fencing, activation/admission, Gateway/startup integration, accounting correctness, or simulator recovery exists. 10. The broad intelligence tree requires reachability, model registry, and shadow-mode evidence before it can affect policy.
11. External integrations need schema/version/freshness/capability contracts and independent failure tests.
12. **Simulation certificates remain non-authoritative research evidence.** The current accepted builder/verifier recomputes embedded runtime/program root hashes, recursively validates bounded CPI forests, constrains deployment/simulation slots to context, requires exact-final wire identity, enforces transaction-version compatibility, and preserves `SUCCESS/FAILURE/UNKNOWN`. Runtime/program hash validation establishes internal consistency only: roots are unkeyed and their defaults/input values are not sourced from an authenticated RPC observation. Certificates do not prove provider authenticity, transaction-byte/message correspondence, signature attestation, landing, finality, or settlement, and remain unwired to execution. The current exact accepted runtime/certificate/test hashes and 16/16 focused test evidence are recorded in research cycle 25; the later review confirmed v1 byte compatibility, v2 full-width feature digests, strict pre-hash input validation, and compatibility's fail-closed hash checks. The creator's local defaults remain illustrative inputs, not evidence that a Solana cluster has those versions/features.

## Research cycle 21 — bounded streaming journal verifier — 2026-10-05

Implemented the first independent code slice from the accepted streaming design in `src/platform/storage/paper-account-v3-integrity.ts`. The verifier now owns or accepts one `BEGIN DEFERRED` snapshot, scalar-preflights the consumed genesis/event BLOBs against the 1 MiB limit before selecting bytes, and scans the account-sequence and generation-sequence axes with synchronous iterators. It validates all rows and uses indexed point lookups rather than event/generation history maps. Returned account summaries and unsupported-family samples are fixed-size; full counts and deterministic digests preserve visibility of omitted tail data. Exact unknown-event row counts feed the command-lifecycle result, so an unknown type after the sample cap remains held. Known-family version variation is marked truncated in either observed order.

An authoring Astra passed isolated TypeScript compilation and fresh-built focused journal/owner/command tests. Independent Astra review accepted the exact repaired journal implementation (source SHA-256 `6372752B0173B46A21F697188621E4981D1076B2488F5B08461157103C26D87E`; journal test SHA-256 `D74764E602AA196F567CC3D6F7D3615625D89A84D8FA4B9C18071D39FAEF0DC8`). It independently passed the 24 focused tests and reproduced/fixed a prior version-order truncation defect. Our full fresh-source `npm run test:all` run passed 378 core, 506 intelligence, 733 platform, and 267 terminal tests (1,884 total, zero failures); log SHA-256 `D09C2FE0F3C69BC09EA09757F81FA39AB44673659CA103FB9B7C28F6EFECF399` at `C:\Users\juans\Documents\Codex\2026-09-30\new-chat\outputs\research-cycle-21-full-current-source-tests.log`. Three pre-existing generated storage modules were restored to their exact pre-run SHA-256 values. The two ordered event queries use the intended indexes with no temporary sort in the inspected Node v24.21.0 / SQLite 3.53.4 schema plan; this remains host/runtime-specific evidence.

The reviewer confirmed that compound corruption can change which first-failure diagnostic is reported compared with the prior materialized verifier (for example, an account-sequence plus generation-sequence gap). Both implementations reject such histories; exact error precedence across simultaneous faults is not preserved and is not a readiness distinction. This slice reduces memory only in the journal verifier: the owner verifier still materializes all events/accounts and history-sized sets, and the command verifier still materializes all lifecycle tables/maps. No full command/owner memory-bound, scratch isolation/quota/supervisor contract, account writer/projection recovery, Gateway/startup integration, or admission capability is delivered. ## Research cycle 26 — Windows process-tree supervision — research/design only — 2026-10-05

Official Microsoft sources support Windows Job Objects as the candidate process-tree boundary for the synchronous SQLite verifier. The sequence should be CreateProcess suspended → assign to a private job with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` → resume; otherwise the worker can spawn an uncontained descendant before assignment. The supervisor must be the only owner of the job handle, wait until the job has zero active processes, and fail closed when host nesting prevents assignment. Default child CreateProcess inheritance joins the job, but breakaway and non-CreateProcess mechanisms require explicit tests. Node's ordinary child kill/detached behavior is not sufficient proof of process-tree death. This finding is recorded in the streaming-verifier design with primary-source links. It is a proposed design, not implemented functionality; independent accuracy review is pending. No release/readiness status changed.

Independent Astra accepted the Windows supervisor design addition at SHA-256 `02FBEDBE94093FC80BA1A454B71177B1F39691A444802F3858A438A319528638`. Review confirmed the suspended-create/assign/resume order, nested-job and breakaway caveats, and the repaired failure rule: wait for the process handle to signal before closing or cleaning; if termination cannot be confirmed, keep verification held and prohibit cleanup/retry. Design review does not accept any implementation; none exists yet.

## Research cycle 27 — RuntimeRoot review and observed-RPC provenance boundary — 2026-10-05

Independent Astra re-reviewed the RuntimeRoot schema-v2/compatibility snapshot and accepted exact source, certificate, schema-test, blueprint-test, execution-chain-test, and design-document hashes recorded in the cycle-25 entry. It confirmed that legacy v1 roots preserve their original ordered preimage and 16-hex digest, while factory-created v2 roots include the full 64-hex feature digest and transaction-version support. The creator checks feature arrays, epoch, context slot, and labels before serialization; the creator still defaults transaction support to `all`, with explicit enum values available to represent a caller's observation. Fresh isolated build and 16/16 focused tests passed. This corrects cycle 23's stale hash values and its statement that `verifyCompatibility()` did not authenticate embedded root hashes; cycle 25/27 supersede those claims.

External protocol research found that Solana's official HTTP RPC reference specifies `getVersion` as returning `solana-core` and an optional numeric `feature-set`, `getGenesisHash` as returning the connected cluster's base58 genesis hash, and `getEpochInfo` as returning `epoch` and `absoluteSlot` ([RPC method index](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http), [`getGenesisHash`](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getgenesishash), [`getEpochInfo`](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getepochinfo)). Agave's feature-gate documentation says the active/inactive set is stored in the Bank and active features update at epoch boundaries ([feature-gate setup](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/wiki/Feature-Gate-Setup-Process)); its source computes runtime `FeatureSnapshot` from an active-feature map ([Agave feature-set source](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/blob/master/feature-set/src/lib.rs)). This is a different concept from the RPC `getVersion.feature-set` build identifier. These are separate endpoint observations; the RPC docs do not establish that a caller-provided feature-name list is a canonical cluster-wide active-feature set or that one provider is trustworthy. Repository call-site search found no production caller of `createRuntimeRoot()`: the current factory's defaults and v2 hash therefore provide deterministic internal commitments, not a captured Solana runtime observation. Next research/implementation boundary: define a typed, provenance-preserving RPC observation envelope that records endpoint identity, commitment/context, request/response hashes, and disagreements across independent providers; derive the RuntimeRoot only from validated observations or leave it unavailable. Do not infer authenticity, feature activation, or authority from hash validity alone.

The per-attempt SQLite identity index is being authored as an isolated, non-integrated candidate. Its current contract is under test with a configurable file page ceiling and canonical owner/lease/lock-nonce UUIDs. Independent review and exact-cap/failure-path tests are still required. It does not provide a filesystem quota, scratch-root ownership or ACL, process-tree supervision, crash cleanup, or verifier integration; the disk and process controls remain open blockers.

## Research cycle 28 — bounded scratch identity index prototype — 2026-10-05

Implemented an isolated `PaperAccountV3ScratchIdentityIndex` candidate for owner/lease/lock-nonce duplicate tracking. The caller must supply a fresh private directory and an explicit `maxBytes` (8 KiB–64 MiB, page-size multiple); the helper derives SQLite `max_page_count`, uses memory journal/temp storage, defensive mode and SQLite length/SQL/ATTACH limits, validates canonical UUID identities, and checks page/file size around each lookup/insert and close. Each insertion is its own autocommit; SQLite errors poison and close the handle. Independent Astra accepted exact source/test/design hashes `3C5A83182BCEBA0D7E00C24DE4B8F35C9C597F1289F8CB05116472C700696CD8`, `2846730E6E27D6258EB992157F8F1F341F0A97C2D6970DED27FDC25C5E161CFD`, and `36E4D7D90B3AE35498C12DB3A8CD9232AA8F96420F031204B953F021B9FC4492`. Primary fresh isolated build, no-emit typecheck, 5 focused tests, and scoped diff check passed. A 256 KiB cap reproduced `SQLITE_FULL`; the rejected identity was absent, the index poisoned, integrity stayed valid, and the file remained at exactly 64 pages with no sidecars.

This is a per-database-file cap and a prototype only. It does not impose a directory/filesystem quota or total process-memory bound; SQLite memory journaling and native cache remain process resources. The caller must discard failed attempt directories, and cleanup requires confirmed worker death. No verifier, Gateway, admission, recovery, or readiness path uses the helper. Remaining blockers are hard storage isolation/quota, protected root lifecycle, cross-platform qualification, process-tree supervision and cleanup, integration with streamed owner verification, and differential/memory/crash tests.

## Research cycle 30 — reject stale RuntimeRoot context — 2026-10-05

The compatibility helper previously tolerated a caller-labeled current RuntimeRoot whose `contextSlot` was older than the certified RuntimeRoot. It now returns `CURRENT_CONTEXT_BEHIND_CERTIFICATE` for that regression while preserving forward context advancement. This is consistent with Solana's `simulateTransaction` lower-bound field `minContextSlot` and the certificate's separate runtime/minimum/simulation slot checks ([official method documentation](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/simulatetransaction)). A regression test proves an observation one slot behind is incompatible; the existing later-slot case remains compatible. Fresh isolated strict TypeScript build and focused runtime/certificate/execution-chain tests passed 16/16, with local no-emit typecheck and scoped diff check. Independent Astra accepted the exact snapshot: runtime source `038E9D8630BDD87A67236CA15B42331C6B3E1F448E0767C6A4A39FEF83B3239C`; schema test `2BD7F2ED6F1343C98C339F53BBF5152094C53E04199F14369DBFC8A35489DE4E`; schema document `E4A8FE85348F6339F915B0E49CA051E0F808622D154D0683CF55228D21750F0A`. Review confirmed a conservative asymmetric freshness rule: no exact slot equality, but older current context cannot establish compatibility with later certified state. This helper has no production callers, so no live execution/readiness behavior changes today; the change tightens only the reusable compatibility contract. Hash validity still does not establish freshness/authenticity of the claimed current observation.

## Research cycle 35 — URL delimiter classification and RuntimeRoot compatibility re-review — 2026-10-05

The final provider-auth review found that WHATWG URL parsing normalizes a trailing empty `?` or `#` to empty `search`/`hash` values. Because MarketHub appends DexScreener route paths to the raw configured base string, `https://api.dexscreener.com?`, `...#`, `/?`, and `/#` can redirect or discard the appended API path while appearing to match the public-read base check. The classifier now rejects literal query/fragment delimiters before assigning `NOT_REQUIRED`, with four regressions. This follows the [WHATWG URL Standard](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/), which distinguishes null and empty query/fragment components even though JavaScript `search`/`hash` getters return an empty string for both. The authoring Astra and independent endpoint reviewer accepted the exact final source/test hashes; they found no remaining endpoint counterexample. This stays a narrow endpoint-contract correction and does not establish authentication for custom URLs.

The separately re-reviewed RuntimeRoot compatibility contract preserves the exact ordered v1 preimage and 16-hex feature digest; v2 factory roots use a 64-hex SHA-256 feature digest, validate input types/shapes before hashing, and bind transaction-version support with default `all`. Independent Astra accepted current runtime source `038E9D8630BDD87A67236CA15B42331C6B3E1F448E0767C6A4A39FEF83B3239C`, certificate source `4CB6BEB2293D86CF426B4DCCFC4B75289E045A111546D8B55E8BB5C8C9852FE5`, and schema test `2BD7F2ED6F1343C98C339F53BBF5152094C53E04199F14369DBFC8A35489DE4E`. It verified legacy/v2 certificate checks and compatibility rules that allow equal/newer context while rejecting a current root behind the certified context. These unkeyed hashes still prove internal consistency only, not RPC authenticity or runtime provenance.

## Research cycle 36 — system health rollup includes lifecycle state — 2026-10-05

A source/response audit found that `/api/system/health` and the shared trust projection labeled `overallStatus` from provider health alone, while returning a separate degraded `operationalState`. This allowed an apparently `NOMINAL` system rollup during lifecycle degradation. The route now preserves the provider dimension as `providerHealthStatus` and derives `overallStatus` from both it and the lifecycle state, using the more severe status. The finite lifecycle mapping treats startup/synchronization/recovery/degraded as DEGRADED; ready/healthy as NOMINAL; open-locked/reduce-only as RESTRICTED; and safety-locked/disconnected/shutting-down as CRITICAL. Unknown values fail closed to DEGRADED. This keeps operator-facing aggregate health aligned with the app's lifecycle while retaining the original provider-specific observation.

This follows the distinction between process liveness and readiness/dependency availability described in the official [Kubernetes probe documentation](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/probes/); the precise severity mapping is a Sylph Fusion policy, not specified by Kubernetes. The regression test exhaustively checks all 60 provider/lifecycle combinations, verifies monotone severity, and probes unknown and prototype-like input keys. Independent reviewer ACCEPTed the final test at SHA-256 `EE4FD7C6128804934482C4AB5D3C20F7BD792F6BCE1828756BD54F60FDD58971`; source SHA-256 `D0B97AD8BA6039590C9E3566637DE60C9408E4DB55BF4D79576E4B228AF0C323`, helper SHA-256 `86DF8430C85227F1F7C76F0BF2DC000D3E7B2423AB25DEBAF9B2B957B336831C`. ## Research cycle 38 — make RPC throttle telemetry truthful in the operator panel — 2026-10-05

The cycle-37 `RpcPool` change added a process-cumulative `localRateLimitDenials` counter and exposed it on endpoint stats. Tracing the integration through `src/fusion.ts` → `RpcEndpointStats` → `/api/soak` → `SoakTelemetry` → `RpcComparisonPanel` showed the terminal was silently dropping the new field. The panel now shows each endpoint's local denial count separately from provider HTTP 429 and generic errors, and gives an aggregate note that states the count is since process start and separate from the 24-hour candidate-drop rate. Prometheus' primary metric guidance defines a counter as monotonically increasing and resettable at process restart, so the UI labels the actual counter's lifetime rather than implying a 24-hour window ([Prometheus metric types](https://prometheus.io/docs/concepts/metric_types/)).

An independent render review exposed three concrete truth failures and the implementation now handles them explicitly: missing/malformed local counts render `Unknown`, partial aggregation says `At least N` and counts endpoints with unavailable telemetry, and only observed zero renders `0`; the 24-hour gate is `UNVERIFIED` unless the source provides an explicit boolean, and a blocked gate no longer invents a 429-specific cause; endpoint health reports `Operational` only for exact `active`, while unsupported statuses render `Unknown`. ## Research cycle 37 — enforce RPC budgets on every pool dispatch path — 2026-10-05

Current source review and independent challenge found that `TokenBucket.acquire()` returning false was ignored by `RpcPool.connection`, so burst traffic beyond the local queue wait could still reach upstream. The reviewer then traced `RpcPool.endpoints` and found a second path: reconciliation and `verifyCluster()` use endpoint-pinned `Connection` objects whose fetch wrapper bypassed both pacing and telemetry. The implementation now routes both pooled failover and every pinned endpoint through one per-endpoint dispatch boundary. Local denials increment `localRateLimitDenials`, do not call `fetch`, do not count as provider calls/errors/drops, and surface a distinct failure when all pooled choices are locally saturated. Direct endpoints remain pinned and propagate their endpoint's failure. Actual HTTP calls, 429s, generic HTTP/transport/JSON-RPC failures, successes, latency, last-success time, and observed slot watermarks are tracked at dispatch.

External primary research supports the constraint: Solana documents shared RPC endpoints may return 429 when usage exceeds provider limits ([Solana RPC overview](https://solana.com/docs/rpc)); IETF RFC 6585 defines 429 as rate limiting and says a response may include `Retry-After` ([RFC 6585 §4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6585#section-4)). This implementation enforces its existing local token budget before transport; it does not yet interpret `Retry-After`, configure provider-specific or method-weighted budgets, coordinate budgets across processes, or strictly suppress pooled dispatch during quarantine. Quarantine currently changes pooled endpoint ranking; direct verification remains endpoint-pinned by design.

The final source and tests were independently ACCEPTed at source SHA-256 `1CA85F99FCEF5C33AB11440668DF8CEFC60DC1E4C705EC7F9AC33935D995E50B` and test SHA-256 `AA51E23F7A5243BBBB717C5A006C32A3A526939FF146BCF6AE741394CE77A030`. The generated `dist/rpc.js` and map were built from that source and synced at SHA-256 `1DC44DE7CBD75CF9DA6F1F0CD10207094835B024B5C9E5A0F3A1D77F5461E8F6` / `AB3AD0B6805002BA9D72DBC7AE32D8AA96B0022D329F3B2AA14E104102315550`. ## Research cycle 39 — make unsupported Solana message versions explicit — 2026-10-05

The saved project-chat artifact `simulation-100-use-coverage.json` identifies its source as “Solana Simulation Evidence” and preserves 100 research requirements; its original `COMPLETION_NOT_PROVEN` values are historical review state, not verified current defects. Cross-checking current source shows partial implementation: the RuntimeRoot and `SimulationCertificate` preserve legacy/v0 compatibility; the simulation verifier is explicitly non-authorizing; transaction artifacts verify exact serialized legacy/v0 message identity; and `TransactionCompatibilityAuthority` separately recognizes v1 RPC observations/resource rules. The production signer artifact parser and simulation certificate still intentionally accept only legacy/v0.

Current official [Solana versioned-transaction documentation](https://solana.com/docs/core/transactions/versioned-transactions) states v1 is active on mainnet, devnet, and testnet. Its `0x81` message prefix, message-level resource config, explicit compute/data limits, and total-lamports priority-fee semantics differ from legacy/v0. The repository pins `@solana/web3.js` 1.98.4 and has no end-to-end v1 build/simulation/signing/reconciliation path. Previously, a v1/unknown version prefix could be reported as a generic SDK decode failure. `decodeSingleSignerMessage` now detects versioned prefixes before SDK deserialization and returns `TRANSACTION_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` for v1 and future versions. This makes the safe boundary observable; it does not add v1 execution support.

The focused transaction, transaction-compatibility, runtime-root, and simulation-certificate suites passed 30/30; the exact-byte coordinator suite passed 9/9 after adding explicit v1/future-prefix denial with zero signer calls. The changed source was compiled in isolation into `dist/platform/execution/transaction-artifact.js`; scoped `git diff --check` passed. No signer or network action occurred. Defect ledger SOL-001 is PARTIALLY_CONTAINED.

The historical “Solana Simulation Evidence” audit also remains only partially connected: `SimulationCertificate` is research-only and carries a locally computed unkeyed digest; no authenticated provider signature, durable raw request/response archive, or production authority connection was verified. Its `cpiGraphHash` hashes a flat `invokedPrograms` sequence, not a parent/child CPI graph. The certified coordinator source explicitly calls itself incomplete, documents steps 6–8 without corresponding methods, and keeps signing/submission quarantined. These are open integration/research gaps, not completed evidence requirements.

Next research should inspect maintained Solana Kit v1 construction/decoding support and plan a separately reviewed compatibility path only after complete wire identity, resource-policy, simulation, signer, RPC, and reconciliation coverage exists. Independently, replace the certificate's flat CPI sequence claim with a versioned trace model derived from requested/validated `innerInstructions`, and persist exact bounded request/response evidence with provenance. Production promotion remains blocked.

## Research cycle 40 — Alert Center rejects archived gate evidence — 2026-10-05

A cross-layer audit found the Alert Center reading the selected `session.rpcHealth` as current even when the soak reader identified its data source as `SESSION LOG READER` and engineRunning was false. The test state contained a historic passed gate while the live RPC status endpoint had no endpoints; this could make a stale archive appear to clear current operations. Missing or malformed fields also defaulted to a pass and zero counters before the cycle 40 changes.

The Alert Center now treats absent/malformed gate data as unverified, only raises the RPC throttling alert when a numeric rate and explicit passing gate are both present, and only reads gate evidence from `liveEngine.rpcHealth` when engineRunning === true. Its regression test confirms an archived passing result is not current evidence. Historical session analysis remains available in Soak Telemetry as session-scoped reporting. The alert does not invent measured zero values.

Verification: alert-center suite 7/7; full terminal suite 281/281; terminal production build succeeded; served app bundle on the existing local UI endpoint contains the new unknown-gate behavior; git diff --check passed. No terminal process was restarted. Defect ledger UI-006 is RESOLVED for this consumer. Soak Telemetry now labels this as a selected-session result and renders unavailable values as unknown. No historical result is represented as permission to run.

Next high-value review: audit SoakTelemetry and OperatorEmergencyView for places where archived status is visually conflated with current status; then resume simulation evidence completeness, including authenticated raw RPC request/response persistence and a real CPI edge model. Production/release authority remains blocked.

## Research cycle 41 — reject disconnected engine state as current evidence — 2026-10-05

A follow-on contract audit found `getSoakTelemetry()` equated any JSON response from the engine state endpoint with `engineRunning: true`. `readRuntimeConfig()` already noticed `connected: false` when labeling its source, but still read limits and mode from the disconnected object. The Alert Center could also consume its `rpcHealth` if the display-level running flag was true. This allowed a responsive process carrying disconnected/stale state to look current.

Added one shared current-engine predicate: a response must not report `connected: false` and must include engine runtime config or limits. `engineRunning` now follows that predicate. `readRuntimeConfig()` uses values from the response only when that predicate passes, and current RPC gate extraction independently rejects an explicitly disconnected response. Regression tests cover archived session approval, connected:false live-engine status, and disconnected limits/mode. This remains observation-only.

## Research cycle 44 — preserve and validate observed CPI stack depth — 2026-10-05

The existing `TxV1TruthEngine` already decoded transaction metadata `innerInstructions`, but it discarded each CPI's reported `stackHeight`. It used `.find()` per top-level instruction, so duplicate groups were silently ignored; groups whose top-level index exceeded the message instruction count were never visited. This kept a potentially useful Solana trace decoder disconnected from any ancestry reconstruction and did not expose when stack depth was missing.

The decoder now preserves `stackHeight` (using explicit null when absent), emits `innerInstructionTraceStatus` as COMPLETE/PARTIAL/UNAVAILABLE, rejects duplicate/out-of-range top-level groups and invalid heights, and limits group count to 256 and instruction count to 16,384. COMPLETE describes reported trace-field coverage only; the output does not infer CPI parent edges or certify execution truth. Null/missing `innerInstructions` is UNAVAILABLE; a returned trace with unknown heights is PARTIAL. TRUTH-001 remains PARTIALLY_CONTAINED because this module has no verified production consumer and has not been connected to SimulationCertificate.

Verification: `npm run build:engine` passed; `test/platform/truth-x.test.mjs` passed 9/9; repository-wide `npm run test:all` passed core 384/384, intelligence 506/506, platform 755/755, terminal 284/284. `git diff --check` passed. SIM-001 remains PARTIALLY_CONTAINED: new certificates label trace unavailable and verify legacy 2.0.0, but no structured graph is yet accepted.

Next: connect only through an explicit adapter that preserves the raw Solana trace, top-level group index, resolved program ID, stack height and source context; reconstruct parent indices only when contiguous known stack levels establish them. The adapter must be bounded and test parsed and compiled response forms. If the evidence is missing, malformed, or incomplete, the certificate stays PARTIAL/UNAVAILABLE. Keep this pipeline non-authorizing until separately reviewed and authenticated provenance exists.

## Research cycle 45 — request and account for simulation CPI trace evidence — 2026-10-05

Official Solana RPC documentation establishes two important response-contract details: `simulateTransaction` supports an `innerInstructions` request option that defaults to false, and its returned inner trace is grouped by top-level instruction index with optional stack heights. The response may contain fully parsed instructions (`parsed`, `program`, `programId`) without `accounts` or `data`, or partially decoded instructions with addresses and base-58 data. The private unsigned simulation observer previously omitted this option, so successful simulations could never produce the trace evidence needed for later ancestry work. Source: [Solana RPC JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures) and [simulateTransaction](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/simulatetransaction).

The observer now explicitly requests `innerInstructions: true`, keeps the exact request and response hashes already used by its receipt, and records a bounded trace summary: COMPLETE/PARTIAL/UNAVAILABLE, group/instruction counts, and a digest of the parsed trace object. It validates both documented instruction encodings, checks program/account addresses, validates group indexes against the decoded message's top-level instruction count, rejects duplicates and malformed parsed instructions, validates optional stack heights, and bounds groups/instructions/serialized trace bytes. Missing/null data remains UNAVAILABLE; missing heights remain PARTIAL. COMPLETE denotes returned field coverage only. This does not reconstruct parent edges, authenticate the provider, persist raw evidence, or authorize signing. Source search found no production caller for the private observer or `SimulationCertificateBuilder`, so the change is currently an isolated research/test capability, not a connected live runtime path. `SimulationCertificate` stays unchanged and continues to report `cpiTraceStatus: UNAVAILABLE` until an independently reviewed graph adapter and durable provenance contract exist.

Verification: `npm run build:engine` passed; `node --test test/platform/unsigned-observation-verifier.test.mjs` passed 71/71, including official parsed/partially-decoded shapes, request-option assertion, complete/partial/unavailable trace cases and malformed group/address rejection. Final repository-wide `npm run test:all` passed: core 384/384, intelligence 506/506, platform 764/764, terminal 284/284. SIM-001 remains PARTIALLY_CONTAINED; no compatibility envelope was changed in this cycle.

Runtime check: the local server on `127.0.0.1:8793` is still PID 36276 and still returns no live endpoints alongside a positive archived-session `rpcHealth.gatePassed`. The loaded handler is stale and requires an operator-approved restart before this API fix becomes active there; no process was restarted during this cycle.

Next research: build a separate normalization adapter that can verify trace ordering and derive parent indexes only from consecutive usable stack heights, then test against official simulation response fixtures. Review its certificate compatibility/versioning before connecting it. Add bounded durable raw request/response evidence with provider/configuration provenance and retention limits. Keep all simulation evidence non-authorizing.

## Research cycle 48 — confirm process-tree limitation in verifier support utility — 2026-10-05

Inspected every repository caller of `runBoundedChildProcess()`: today it is used only by the Windows protected scratch-root provider. That provider invokes a fixed PowerShell script with no child process creation, so its current direct-child timeout handling is bounded to the present implementation. However, the helper's contract explicitly kills only its direct child, while the saved v3 verifier design requires a private Windows Job Object, suspended process assignment, kill-on-close, and process/job exit proof before scratch cleanup. Official Microsoft documentation confirms Job Objects manage associated processes as a group, normally include CreateProcess descendants unless breakaway rules apply, and support `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`; `AssignProcessToJobObject` documents suspended creation as the mechanism when setup must precede execution. The helper must therefore remain forbidden for the future verifier supervisor; no safe pure-Node drop-in can establish the required containment. This is a verified design boundary, not a newly discovered defect in the current fixed PowerShell caller. No code was changed. Sources: [Microsoft Job Objects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/job-objects), [AssignProcessToJobObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/jobapi2/nf-jobapi2-assignprocesstojobobject), [Node child_process](https://nodejs.org/download/release/v24.21.0/docs/api/child_process.html).

## Research cycle 52 — validate distribution transportability evidence and currency stress

The previous cycle's note that `npm run build:engine` was blocked is stale: on the current source snapshot the engine build passes. External research reinforces that distribution-shift calibration depends on shift assumptions and temporal dependence: [Park et al., PMLR 2020](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v108/park20b.html) study calibrated prediction under covariate shift, while [Huang et al., PMLR 2026](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v337/huang26b.html) address online calibration under temporal dependence and nonstationarity. These papers do not validate Sylph's numeric constants.

## Research cycle 54 — revise the CPI trace contract from Agave execution semantics

The project chat history and cycle 45 identified the missing `innerInstructions: true` request; cycle 47 produced the initial compatibility proposal. The current observer now requests the field, bounds and hashes it, and records top-level instruction count, but its `COMPLETE` enum means stack-height field coverage only. It does not establish valid parent edges or provider completeness. The certificate still emits schema `2.1.0` and marks the structured trace unavailable.

Official Solana RPC documentation confirms simulations return parsed or partially decoded instructions and `innerInstructions` is null unless recording is requested. Agave's transaction-status source shows `stack_height` is optional `u32`; current and v2.3.13 transaction processors retain instruction order, while group construction sorts by top-level index and filters empty groups. The independent Astra Protocol review found that the draft's parent algorithm should be more exact: groups are sparse but strictly ordered, empty groups are noncanonical; valid stack heights begin at 2, may hold or rise only one level, and may fall to any height ≥2; repeated siblings and repeated program IDs are valid. If a group contains an unknown height, suppress all parent edges for that group. Agave currently uses call-stack depth 5 or 9 with SIMD-0268, but appends a CPI trace before checking depth, so a failed CallDepth attempt can be reported one level above the configured successful depth. Parser ceilings must remain resource policy, not protocol limits. Sources: [Solana simulation RPC](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/simulatetransaction), [Solana JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures#simulation-results), [Agave v2.3.13 trace extraction](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/blob/v2.3.13/svm/src/transaction_processor.rs#L920), [current Agave group construction](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/blob/master/transaction-status/src/lib.rs#L120), [execution budget](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/blob/master/program-runtime/src/execution_budget.rs#L5), and [transaction-context push](https://github.com/anza-xyz/agave/blob/master/transaction-context/src/transaction.rs#L446).

The revised proposal now uses `STRUCTURALLY_VALID`, narrowly meaning all reported nodes have validated fields and deterministic parents; it explicitly cannot claim that a provider returned every executed CPI. It defines exact canonicalization/hash domains, nested/sibling/unwind vectors, feasibility across missing heights, unavailable/missing/null/empty distinctions, disjoint simulation instruction unions, and receipt-bound construction. Astra Protocol and Astra Verification accepted the design at exact proposal SHA-256 `2BD62756754B397B17210D9220792039CBBAB8098B33BBBDB48A067828E32F85`; this is design acceptance only. ## Research cycle 55 — bound and independently verify normalized CPI payloads — 2026-10-05

Implemented the accepted normalization-v1 proposal as an isolated, non-authorizing CPI trace normalizer/verifier. It accepts only Solana's parsed or partially decoded instruction unions; validates exact plain data without invoking accessors; snapshots bounded source fields; recomputes per-instruction digests; distinguishes requested/missing/null/empty evidence; validates sparse ordered groups and feasible stack paths; and emits parents only for groups with fully known heights. Verification rebuilds the trace from retained source fields and checks status, parents, instruction digests, and the trace digest. Unkeyed hashes prove internal consistency only; they do not authenticate an RPC or establish completeness.

The independent review found and drove fixes for two resource-bound defects: full normalized node accounting originally happened after rebuilding groups, and encoded byte limits were checked after constructing potentially oversized canonical strings. Verification now preflights aggregate source and complete wrapper nodes, and incrementally measures UTF-8 canonical bytes before hashing/rebuilding. The 2 MB ceiling is consistently on the trace preimage; the fixed 64-hex digest envelope is validated separately and is included in the full-payload node count. A near-limit vector confirms the preimage can be just under 2,000,000 bytes while its serialized hash envelope crosses that size and still verifies. A malformed 2.4 MB parent, oversized aggregate source fields, accessors, inconsistent content/digests, and source-under/full-node-over payloads are rejected.

Astra Verification ACCEPTed the exact isolated implementation: source SHA-256 `F001BF6DBB1BEB77404E75214B63FA3161152105E9E04043A0C639C30D03D691`, compiled JS SHA-256 `3AC7F7842D630C0C6EB54A3B40DE7C70E114DB542D5C5AD4DDEB0DA2146CFB8C`, and focused test SHA-256 `324BE7E56331A89F0156D21EE5386248345CADCFD4638D307E6C12D25F528C60`. Isolated module type-check passed and normalizer plus frozen local legacy compatibility tests passed 18/18. A platform suite passed 786/786 after the full-node preflight addition and before the final byte-cap refinement. After unrelated untracked startup-audit/canary source files appeared in the shared worktree, `npm run build:engine` began failing on their missing module/stale API references; a later full platform run also failed while importing their generated startup audit. Those files were preserved and not edited in this cycle, so current repository-wide verification remains blocked by that concurrent worktree state. `git diff --check` reports no whitespace errors.

The schema proposal remains unintegrated. `SimulationCertificateBuilder` has no production caller, the unsigned observer remains non-authorizing, and construction still lacks a private receipt-bound adapter. Local 2.0/2.1 fixtures verify only a frozen local candidate; released historical producer compatibility remains unverified. Subsequent build and full-suite checks are recorded in cycle 56.

No log observation is promoted into chain-context evidence by this cycle. Current official Solana `logsSubscribe` notifications contain context slot and transaction signature/log data, but no blockhash. The official `blockSubscribe` response contains blockhash and transactions but the method is marked unstable and requires validator configuration. Yellowstone's protocol similarly separates a transaction update (slot and transaction info) from block updates (slot, blockhash, transaction membership). Therefore a safe join must match the exact signature inside a block's transaction list, with provider/commitment/fork provenance retained; slot equality alone is insufficient. Sources: [Solana logsSubscribe](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/websocket/logssubscribe), [Solana blockSubscribe](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/websocket/blocksubscribe), [Yellowstone Geyser protobuf](https://github.com/rpcpool/yellowstone-grpc/blob/master/yellowstone-grpc-proto/proto/geyser.proto).

The runtime-divergence hook also compared two identical hard-coded decision vectors immediately before a buy; it could only record fake parity. That call was removed because neither the legacy decision vector nor a shadow decision vector is actually produced at that point. The auditor must remain disconnected until both are derived from the same candidate generation and exact state snapshot.

Verification: `npm run build:engine` passed. Focused startup-audit, canary, runtime-divergence, CPI-normalizer, and frozen local-legacy tests passed 25/25 after removing the synthetic parity call. The complete `npm run test:all` passed with exit code 0; the output reported 393/393 core tests and 285/285 terminal tests, with no suite failure. `git diff --check` passed. Existing uncommitted and untracked work was preserved. The normalizer remains an isolated non-authorizing module: certificate/observer receipt-bound integration and released historical 2.0/2.1 provenance are still absent.

Next research: design a bounded feed-to-block membership join with explicit pending, matched, missing, ambiguous, and retracted states; exact `(provider, slot, signature, blockhash)` identity; capped pending queues/expiry; reconnect and fork rollback coverage; and raw-source digests. Use a supported block stream or indexed block fetch, not unstable `blockSubscribe` as an assumed universal capability. Only a matching block-membership observation may supply bank hash context, and provider data remains an observation rather than cryptographic proof. Separately continue the independently reviewed receipt-bound CPI certificate bridge; keep the current trace evidence non-authorizing.

## Research cycle 57 — select a fork-context integration compatible with the pinned Windows client — 2026-10-06

Compared the project's actual `@triton-one/yellowstone-grpc` 4.0.2 dependency and generated declarations with the current upstream SDK/protocol. The pinned 4.0.2 schema already carries `SubscribeUpdateBlock.blockhash` and a transaction list, but its transaction/block/block-meta types do not expose the newer `bankId`. Therefore the current supported way to enrich a transaction observation is to match its exact signature and slot against a block update from the same provider stream and retain that block's hash; a same-slot-only merge remains forbidden. This requires a provider capability check, resource budget for full transaction lists, and explicit handling for absent blocks, duplicate/conflicting memberships, reconnects, and late fork changes.

The current upstream `@triton-one/yellowstone-grpc` 8.0.0 adds `bankId` to transaction and block updates and offers bank-aware reconnect events. Its README restricts reconnect recovery to processed commitment and no startup snapshot/from-slot; it also says recovery buffers updates without a size cap while waiting for finalized winner evidence. Its npm package lists optional N-API binaries for macOS and Linux, but none for Windows. The repository runs on Windows and currently consumes confirmed updates, so upgrading directly to 8.0.0 would neither permit its reconnect mode under the current commitment nor provide a Windows native binary; it risks breaking the existing feed. The dependency was not changed. Sources: [Yellowstone 8.0.0 package](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@triton-one/yellowstone-grpc/v/8.0.0), [Yellowstone repository release history](https://github.com/rpcpool/yellowstone-grpc/releases), [Yellowstone protocol definitions](https://github.com/rpcpool/yellowstone-grpc/blob/master/yellowstone-grpc-proto/proto/geyser.proto).

Next: prototype and review an optional bounded block-membership correlator against the current v4 wire schema before changing the live subscription. Measure block message sizes and provider support without allowing the evidence stream to disrupt the transaction feed. Keep its outputs as observation provenance; do not promote them to authenticated truth or trading authority.

## Research cycle 60 — connect discovery and point-in-time snapshots to durable storage — 2026-10-05

The Engine now retains the exact `RawObservationEnvelope` attached to a create event and writes `candidate_discovered_v1` to the existing worker-owned SQLite `audit` table. Feature snapshots write `candidate_snapshot_v1` with both the stable `candidateGenerationId` and the snapshot's existing evidence-state `candidateId`; this preserves the distinction between an entity's identity and a snapshot whose evidence can change. Snapshot rows include the source envelope and feature-seal hash and are persisted even when the optional `SESSION_DIR` file logger is absent. The `Store` API validates event names, bounds payloads at 65,536 bytes and reads at 10,000 rows, and worker queue rejection is surfaced as `research_observation_persist_failed` without blocking paper decisions or exits.

The Engine also persists `candidate_restriction_v1`, `candidate_entry_gates_passed_v1`, `candidate_submission_blocked_v1`, `candidate_order_build_started_v1`, `candidate_order_build_failed_v1`, `candidate_order_built_v1`, `candidate_order_build_abandoned_v1`, and `candidate_paper_fill_v1`. Random attempt UUIDs join gate-pass, build, and result records; an in-memory sequence is retained only as a readable per-candidate entry-attempt number. The stable `candidateGenerationId` is now retained as an optional position field, preserving old state compatibility while linking sell attempts after candidate eviction and Store reopen. Built results retain quote time, quoted output and modeled overhead when supplied by the executor. Paper fills are labeled `PAPER_SIMULATED_FILL_NOT_CHAIN_EVIDENCE` and linked to the candidate generation; this does not mean real submission or settlement happened. E2E coverage includes a failed build then successful retry, fill attribution across entry/partial/full exit, position identity after Store reopen, and snapshot persistence without a session logger.

The audit table is ordinary append-only application storage with WAL/FULL settings, not a hash chain or tamper-proof journal; it can be pruned and local database mutation is outside this integrity guarantee. Persisting these rows is asynchronous and does not block paper decisions; sustained queue overload can cause failed writes, which are logged but not retried. There is no backpressure, durable retry spool, completeness certificate, or verified immutable research export. Real submission outcomes, chain reconciliation, finalized settlement, and authenticated provider evidence remain outside this paper-only integration.

Verification: `npm run build:engine` passed; focused Store and Engine lifecycle tests passed 5/5; final `npm run test:all` passed with core 394, intelligence 541, platform 795, and terminal 285 tests (2,015 total; zero failures). `git diff --check` passed. The repository contains pre-existing dirty and untracked changes from other audit work; they were preserved.

Next: define a restart-safe retention and completeness/export contract, and test how production captures and surfaces storage failure/queue overload. Then connect actual submitted transactions to finalized chain evidence with explicit unresolved states; do not merge descriptive CSV outcomes with these app-side records until identities and missingness are reconciled.

## Research cycle 61 — expose dropped research writes in runtime health — 2026-10-05

Rechecked the actual Store/Engine call path and the default runtime: `Store` dispatches through a bounded 128-request worker queue; `persistResearchObservation()` is intentionally fire-and-forget so it cannot stall paper fills or exits; failures were logged but absent from the Engine health snapshot and soak checkpoint. The Engine now counts research persistence failures in memory, retains the latest failure time/event/reason, and exposes both from `Engine.snapshot()` (the dashboard's `/api/state` source) and `emitCheckpoint()`. A regression forces an audit write rejection and verifies the health view updates. This is operational visibility, not proof of complete capture; counters reset on process restart and the failing storage cannot durably store its own failure.

Primary-source durability check: SQLite documents that `synchronous=FULL` in WAL mode syncs the WAL after each transaction commit and provides ACID durability under the OS/filesystem contract. The repository already sets WAL and `synchronous=FULL` in its generation-storage open path. That protects writes which the worker actually commits; it cannot recover events rejected by the application queue or a process that never awaited persistence. Source: [SQLite PRAGMA synchronous](https://sqlite.org/pragma.html), [SQLite WAL](https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html).

Verification: `npm run build:engine` passed; Store and Engine focused tests passed 5/5; final `npm run test:all` passed (core 394, intelligence 541, platform 795, terminal 285; zero failures); `git diff --check` passed. ## Research cycle 62 — retain observed research-evidence loss across restart — 2026-10-05

The cycle 61 in-memory loss counter disappeared on process restart. Added optional `State.researchEvidenceLoss` with a versioned count, first/last timestamps, bounded event/reason identifiers, and an explicit recovery-required bit. Older stored state remains valid because the field is optional. The Engine restores only strictly validated markers; a malformed marker reports an unknown count and `INVALID` status rather than being treated as zero loss. Newly observed failures set an in-memory marker, and ordinary Engine state saves carry it forward. Health surfaces distinguish `NONE_RECORDED`, `PENDING_STATE_SAVE`, `PERSISTED`, and `INVALID`. No side-channel state write is started by the asynchronous evidence-failure callback, because queuing an older full-state snapshot could overwrite newer economic state.

This is a durable *known-loss marker*, not a payload spool or proof that the evidence stream is complete. A crash before the next successful state save loses the in-memory marker. Reopening after a persisted marker correctly retains the count and requires recovery; no automatic clear or evidence-recovery workflow exists. The audit/audit-event path is still outside the tamper-evident capital ledger.

External storage research confirmed the repository already exposes a live SQLite snapshot through `Store.backup()` using `VACUUM INTO`; SQLite documents this as a consistent database copy. Therefore a second backup implementation is not justified. The missing work is a versioned export manifest that hashes the snapshot, binds its event sequence/coverage window, reports pruned or known-missing ranges, and can be independently verified. SQLite consistency does not by itself make an export immutable, complete, or externally anchored. Sources: [SQLite Online Backup API and live database backup](https://www.sqlite.org/backup.html), [SQLite corruption and safe-backup guidance](https://www.sqlite.org/howtocorrupt.html).

Verification: `npm test` passed the engine build and all four test groups (2,015 tests total: core 394, intelligence 541, platform 795, terminal 285; zero failures). The Engine settlement/evidence end-to-end file passed 2/2 after the fixture-local recovery-store name collision was corrected; it verifies pending-to-persisted state transition, Store reopen/Engine reconstruction, and invalid-marker handling. `git diff --check` passed after the final documentation update. Existing user changes were preserved.

Next: complete current verification, then add the export manifest and an independent verifier against the existing SQLite snapshot. Keep loss counts explicitly separate from missing-event counts: a count of failed writes cannot identify all missing sequence ranges or prove that no process crash truncated a session.

## Research cycle 63 — prototype a bounded Yellowstone v4 block-membership correlator — 2026-10-05

Implemented `BlockMembershipCorrelator` as an isolated observation adapter for joining a transaction-log observation with an exact signature in a same-provider, same-slot block update. The join retains commitment, blockhash, source payload digests, and explicit `UNVERIFIED`/no-bank-ID labels. It supports transaction-first and block-first arrival, positive membership, conservative `MISSING` only when a complete-list assertion at adequate commitment is supplied, conflicting block contexts, transaction conflicts, retractions, expiry, and explicit queue/signature overload. Identical replays are idempotent across observation IDs when payload digest and membership agree. Bounded conflict-table saturation fails closed for the retention window rather than dropping ambiguity and resuming confident joins.

Compared the implementation against the pinned `@triton-one/yellowstone-grpc` 4.0.2 generated block protobuf. A signature-only wire-size fixture scales with 100 versus 1,000 transaction entries and stays below 100 KB for 1,000 entries; this is a synthetic lower-bound sizing check, not a live provider measurement (transaction/meta/account payloads are omitted). The current `Feed.geyser()` remains unchanged and does not request block updates. Thus no provider capability, actual block completeness, message-size ceiling, reconnect/replay behavior, or Engine-to-correlator integration is established. The correlator is not exported into a live decision path and cannot create a `FusionEnvelopeV2`, bank identity, canonicality claim, or trading authority. Official Yellowstone schema separates transaction and block updates and current upstream has since added `bank_id` fields; that newer schema does not change the pinned v4 contract. Solana's `getBlock` also returns confirmed block transaction data and remains an alternative for a separately bounded indexed-fetch adapter. Sources: [pinned Yellowstone 4.0.2 package](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@triton-one/yellowstone-grpc/v/4.0.2), [Yellowstone protocol](https://github.com/rpcpool/yellowstone-grpc/blob/master/yellowstone-grpc-proto/proto/geyser.proto), [Yellowstone changelog](https://github.com/rpcpool/yellowstone-grpc/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md), [Solana RPC methods](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http).

Verification: repository TypeScript build passed; the focused correlator suite passed 11/11, including exact-membership ordering, provider/slot/commitment scoping, replay idempotency, transaction and fork conflicts, fail-closed conflict-capacity saturation, complete-list absence semantics, retraction, expiry and bounds, pinned-protobuf signature-only sizing, and rejected oversized updates with no pending transactions. The complete `npm run test:all` passed: core 394, intelligence 541, platform 806, and terminal 285 (2,026 total; zero failures). `git diff --check` passed. No live feed request, configuration, or trading behavior changed.

Next: independently review the prototype's compatibility and uncertainty rules; then, if accepted, add an opt-in provider capability probe or a bounded `getBlock` adapter that measures real serialized message size and tests reconnect/fork/retraction behavior. Integrate the correlator only behind evidence-only outputs after the provider's complete block-list contract is verified. Continue full-suite verification against the current shared worktree and then build the existing SQLite snapshot export manifest without conflating failed-write counts with missing-event ranges.

## Research cycle 64 — remove unverified block-context joins from the Engine — 2026-10-06

Re-audited the actual current runtime after cycle 63 and found a concurrent, unfinished integration in `Engine`: every feed event was sent to `BlockContextJoiner`, which retained observations and could produce `FusionEnvelopeV2` from caller-supplied block evidence, while the live feed still supplies transaction logs only. The Engine path had no source of block membership, and its `MarketEvent` did not guarantee agreement between outer slot/signature fields and the embedded `RawObservationEnvelope`. This contradicted the earlier description that the joiner was disconnected. Removed the joiner from Engine construction and `onEvent`, leaving the helper explicitly standalone. Updated the component registry to describe the actual feed transports and disconnected pipeline accurately.

Hardened the standalone helper as well: it requires outer event slot/signature to match the embedded observation, checks raw-payload hash and commitment, and refuses to combine a stronger transaction commitment with a weaker block context. Astra's review identified shallow nested payload mutation, count-only memory bounds, sparse membership arrays, getter/proxy time-of-check races, custom JSON hooks, and extra/unbounded fields. The helper now snapshots only allowlisted identity records, rejects accessors and proxies, validates dense bounded arrays, manually copies a bounded JSON-safe payload (with explicit Solana `PublicKey` and BN handling), enforces per-event and aggregate pending-byte limits, and rejects changed fork lineage or commitment downgrade on duplicate block reports. Regression tests cover nested mutation, huge payloads, custom `toJSON`, sparse arrays, extra fields, accessors/proxies, weaker commitment, and lineage conflict. `connectionGeneration` explicitly permits `unknown` rather than encoding missing knowledge as generation zero. The evidence label remains `SOURCE_REPORTED_BLOCK_MEMBERSHIP_ONLY`; this is not provider authentication, bank identity verification, or canonicality.

After the follow-up changes, a final independent re-review was requested and is pending. Until that review completes, the earlier REVISE verdict governs: keep the helper only as an isolated, unwired experiment. Provider ID, block payload digest, and bank label remain source assertions and do not establish authenticated provider identity or verified chain facts.
