## Research cycle 123 — compare finalized transaction metadata independently — 2026-10-07

Followed the prior cycle's open landed-evidence question through `RpcPool.auditFinalizedBlockRange` and its provider comparison. The read-only auditor fetched `getBlock` with `transactionDetails: full`, but its fingerprint included only blockhash, parent linkage, signatures and version; execution `meta` was discarded. Two configured providers could therefore agree on block identity while disagreeing on a transaction's reported error, fee, balances, loaded addresses or inner CPI trace, yet still receive `MATCHED_CONFIGURED_ENDPOINTS`. Solana documents full block metadata separately from signed transaction identity, and marks `innerInstructions` unavailable when null or omitted. Sources: [Solana `getBlock`](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getblock), [Solana RPC JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures).

Added a separate, bounded canonical JSON digest for each non-null transaction metadata object, preserving array order while sorting object keys and rejecting proxies, accessors, cycles, non-JSON values, excessive depth, and over-budget structures. The per-block transaction-metadata digest binds each canonical metadata object to the corresponding signature/version. Provider comparison now reports `transactionMetadataStatus` and distinct metadata fingerprints; if all endpoints provide complete metadata and those fingerprints disagree, the slot status is `DISAGREEMENT` even when block fingerprints match. Missing metadata yields `PARTIAL` or `UNAVAILABLE`, never a match. The metadata digest is not folded into the block identity fingerprint and carries no proof or execution authority.

History cross-check: prior “Upgrade sylph-fusion with Sol” work already repaired out-of-order stream watermark recovery, and the upgrade histories documented preserving dirty work and strict observation/authority separation. This cycle does not repeat those changes. The SOL-SYLPH exit research remains shadow-only and still requires chronological cost-aware holdout evidence; no exit or capital logic changed here.

Verification: the complete `npm test` command exited 0 (core 410, intelligence 561, terminal 306, no reported failures); the finalized-block focused suite passed 15/15, including same-block/different-meta disagreement, key-order stability, and getter rejection. Engine build passed. Local `HEAD` and GitHub `main` are both `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`; current public API reports zero issues and zero PRs. The working tree contains pre-existing unrelated modifications; they remain intact. No live provider request, signing, submission, commit or push occurred.

Remaining: RPC metadata can be endpoint-fabricated or incomplete, and canonical hashing does not validate Solana ledger execution or resolve CPI account indexes against static and lookup-table keys. The next upgrade should expose bounded per-transaction evidence for an explicitly requested signature, resolve account indexes including loaded addresses, and keep provider quorum and finalized commitment as reported observations until an independent raw account/economic reconciliation exists. The UI's local paper simulator remains separate from chain-observation receipts.

## Research cycle 124 — opt-in signature-specific finalized CPI evidence — 2026-10-07

Continued from cycle 123 by tracing the installed `@solana/web3.js` decoder as used by `RpcPool.getBlock`, then compared its result shape with current Solana RPC documentation. `getBlock` full transaction responses are converted to legacy `Message` or `MessageV0` objects; v0 messages provide static account keys, while `meta.loadedAddresses` supplies writable then readonly lookup-resolved addresses. Compiled inner instructions carry account/program indexes and base58 instruction data. This allows deterministic address resolution without issuing account-table requests. Sources: [Solana `getBlock`](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getblock), [Solana RPC JSON structures: inner instructions and loaded addresses](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures), [installed web3.js decoder](../../node_modules/@solana/web3.js/lib/index.cjs.js).

Added optional `transactionSignature` to `auditFinalizedBlockRange`/`RpcPool.auditFinalizedBlockRange`. When supplied, each available block searches only the transaction whose first signature matches, then returns bounded CPI nodes with resolved program/account addresses, instruction-data hashes, stack heights, and reconstructed parent links. V0 resolution checks lookup counts against the message's table index counts and refuses to invent missing addresses. The trace caps inner nodes, account references, depth and encoded size; validates canonical base58 payloads, index ranges, ordered groups and feasible stack paths; and freezes the returned evidence snapshot. Statuses distinguish not-in-block, absent metadata, absent trace, missing account keys, partial stack heights and malformed traces. Cross-provider targeted trace hashes are compared separately, and a mismatch surfaces as a slot disagreement. This remains a read-only provider observation; it does not verify signatures, prove a provider's CPI report, authorize recovery, or settle economics.

Verification: the engine build passed and the focused finalized-block suite passed 17/17, covering v0 static + lookup key ordering, nested parent reconstruction, data hashing, partial/unavailable distinction, missing lookup evidence, malformed signatures, cross-provider disagreement, and the public RpcPool option forwarding. The full repository `npm test` exited 0: core 410, intelligence 561, platform 895, terminal 306 (2,172 total). Public GitHub `main` and local HEAD are both `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`; GitHub currently lists only `main`, zero open issues and zero open PRs. The worktree's existing unrelated edits remain preserved; no transaction was sent and no commit/push was made.

Remaining: this is not yet connected to a caller that stores or reconciles targeted traces against finalized raw account deltas. A single provider can fabricate internally consistent RPC data; even quorum is only configured-endpoint agreement. Keep the observer disconnected from production authority and the paper-terminal UI. Next, audit how signature-specific evidence should be persisted and reconciled without promoting incomplete or provider-reported values to settlement truth.

Follow-up audit: the certificate design already requires transaction outcome and trace structure to remain separate, and explicitly limits its current observer to successful simulation traces. The finalized-block reader now carries the RPC-reported `meta.err` as `transactionError` with `transactionOutcome` (`REPORTED_SUCCESS`, `REPORTED_FAILURE`, or `UNAVAILABLE`); only literal `null` indicates provider-reported success, and omission is never coerced. Error JSON is bounded, canonically snapshotted, detached from provider-owned objects, and frozen before it is included in the trace hash. Slot comparison now reports `PARTIAL` when a requested CPI trace is partial, even when every configured provider has the same partial observation. This improves evidence labeling only: outcome and trace remain provider-controlled metadata, not execution proof. Exact-source full `npm test` passed: core 410, intelligence 561, platform 896, terminal 306 (2,173 total); focused finalized-block suite passed 18/18. The existing RuntimeRoot schema-v2 full feature-set hash and epoch/context/feature-list validation were confirmed by platform regression tests and left unchanged.

Before durable integration, compare the raw evidence-store contract against the existing proposal `docs/audit/cpi-trace-certificate-v2-proposal.md`: bind the exact transaction/message identity, request and response digests, provider/configuration provenance, finalized slot, and evidence receipt; define bounded retention and replay; then independently reconcile reported instructions with transaction outcome, account keys, token/SOL balance deltas, and program logs. Do not reuse the simulation receipt or its proposed schema as a landed-transaction attestation. A trace may be structurally complete and still be incomplete or fabricated by its provider.

## Research cycle 125 — account for WSOL in economic transaction deltas — 2026-10-07

Rechecked the current asset-delta implementation against its existing focused test, nearby reconciliation code, prior “Upgrade sylph-fusion with Sol” and SOL-SYLPH exit research, and the finalized CPI evidence cycle above. Prior trading research supports exact, cost-aware observed outcomes but did not validate a new entry/exit rule; no strategy, capital setting, or live authority changed. Current Solana JSON-structure documentation defines transaction `preBalances`/`postBalances` as account-indexed lamports and `preTokenBalances`/`postTokenBalances` as separate token-balance snapshots with raw integer amounts serialized as strings. Sources: [Solana RPC JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures), [Solana transaction introspection](https://solana.com/docs/core/transactions/transaction-introspection).

Current-state cross-check: local `main` and GitHub `main` both resolve to `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`; GitHub lists zero open issues and zero open PRs. The existing paper runtime is still answering on loopback port 8793 with `liveReady:false`, `liveSigningUnavailable:true`, `productionCapitalAuthorityBlocked:true`, and 8/10 gates passed. This change did not restart or mutate that process.

The current Solana transaction-introspection guide describes `@solana/transaction-introspection` as an address-resolving CPI-tree walker and notes v1 decoding requires `@solana/kit` 7.0. This repository still pins `@solana/web3.js` 1.98.4 and deliberately keeps the production signer/reconciler at legacy/v0; cycle 39 already recorded that incomplete v1 path. The package is therefore a candidate for a separately reviewed read-only decoder/compatibility study, not a reason to widen transaction-version claims or install a new dependency in this WSOL-accounting fix.

The audit found that `AssetDeltaEngine` computed wallet WSOL pre/post token balances but omitted their difference from `netEconomicSolDelta`, despite its comment promising that movement would be included. It also set `isWSOLWrapped` true when a wallet merely held WSOL, even if the transaction did not change that balance. The engine now exposes `wsolDelta`, includes it in the combined native-SOL/WSOL economic delta before fee/tip isolation, and reports WSOL activity only when that balance changes. It now requires an explicit successful `meta.err === null`, safe and complete account-indexed lamport arrays, present token-balance arrays, and canonical unsigned decimal token amount strings before producing a delta report. This makes missing or precision-losing inputs fail closed instead of generating a plausible number.

Focused certified-execution/asset-delta tests passed 9/9, including unchanged pre-existing WSOL, changed WSOL, missing outcome, unsafe lamports, and noncanonical token amounts. Exact-source full `npm test` exited 0: core 410, intelligence 561, platform 898, terminal 306 (2,175 total); engine build passed. `git diff --check` is recorded after the final audit pass. Existing unrelated worktree edits were preserved; no network transaction, commit, or push occurred.

Remaining gaps: the new tip and ATA funding fields are derived from provider-reported instruction metadata, not an authenticated provider receipt. `innerInstructions: null`, malformed instruction metadata, or missing account keys cannot prove CPI completeness; the Jito status remains `PARTIAL` in that case. ATA funding is only reported when a unique System Program `Create` instruction identifies the wallet as payer and the resulting address matches the canonical associated token address for the wallet, mint, and token program; otherwise the value is `UNAVAILABLE`. This is exact create funding, not an independently verified rent-exemption quote or proof that a token-account creation was economically required. CPI trace evidence must still be reconciled against a finalized transaction and independent provider evidence before settlement authority. The separate simulation trace proposal must not be reused as a landed-transaction attestation.

Next highest-value review: design a durable landed-transaction evidence receipt that binds message identity, request/response digests, provider/configuration provenance, finalized slot, execution outcome, and complete account-indexed native/token deltas, with bounded retention and replay. Keep it separate from simulation certificates and keep live signing/submission quarantined until independent review and end-to-end reconciliation pass.

## Research cycle 126 — instruction-bound tip and ATA funding attribution — 2026-10-07

Continued cycle 125's attribution audit after inspecting the current source, tests, adjacent reconciliation pathway, the official Solana RPC JSON shape and transaction-introspection documentation, and prior “Upgrades”, “Sol”, “SOL_SYLPH”, and “Solana Simulation Evidence” project chats. This change did not restart it or contact a Solana RPC.

The previous Jito tip estimator summed configured tip-account balance increases, which could misattribute transfers made by another transaction participant. It now decodes outer and provider-reported inner System Program `Transfer` / `TransferWithSeed` instructions and counts only transfers whose source is the wallet and destination is configured. Tip evidence reports `PARTIAL` when inner instructions or other instruction visibility is unavailable or malformed, and does not infer tips from balance deltas. The previous fixed 2,039,280-lamport ATA guess has been removed from this report path. Funding is reported only for a unique System Program `Create` paid by this wallet when the new wallet-owned target-mint token account matches the canonical associated address for its token program; ambiguous, third-party-funded, non-ATA, or insufficiently described cases return `UNAVAILABLE`. When proven, the exact creation funding is also added back to `netEconomicSolDelta`, excluding account setup from trade proceeds; unresolved funding is left in the native balance delta and labeled unavailable. This is instruction-reported creation funding, not a rent-exemption calculation or authenticated settlement proof.

Added regression coverage for a wallet-funded configured tip, a tip-account increase funded by another participant, missing inner-instruction visibility, exact ATA derivation and create funding, wrong payer, unchanged WSOL, missing transaction outcome, unsafe account balances, and malformed token amounts. The engine build passed; focused certified-execution tests passed 12/12; full `npm test` exited 0 with core 410/410, intelligence 561/561, platform 898/898, terminal 306/306 (2,175 total). `git diff --check` passed. No live transaction, signer invocation, commit, or push was performed. The worktree retains 157 changed paths that predate or extend beyond this narrow fix; they were not reverted or normalized.

Remaining: provider-reported outer/inner instructions are not authenticated evidence and CPI completeness depends on RPC metadata. The execution-delta component has no independent provider receipt or durable reconciliation link yet. Before such evidence can inform settlement, separately design and review a landed-transaction receipt binding exact message identity, request/response digests, provider configuration, finalized slot, outcome, and account-indexed deltas; do not reuse the simulation-only CPI certificate proposal. The latest local service probe remains paper-only and blocked from production signing/capital. Next research pass should map finalized block evidence into the existing reconciliation chain and verify there is no authority bypass, then obtain independent compatibility review before accepting any schema/API widening.

## Research cycle 127 — bind legacy reconciliation to matching finalized observations — 2026-10-07

**Repository truth.** The active worktree is `main` at `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`, and GitHub's API currently reports only `main` at that SHA, zero open issues, and zero open pull requests. The local paper terminal still responds as `sylph-paper-terminal` in `paper` mode. Its Node process predates these source/build changes and was not restarted, so this upgrade is tested but not runtime-connected. Live signing and submission remain quarantined. The full user-provided 5,166-line Capturable Multiplier Blueprint was read in line-bounded sections and applied as project requirements; its trading-data constraints agree with the October token report: maxima are descriptive, not achievable fills.

**Three investigations / findings.** (1) Source tracing from `fusion.ts` through `LiveExecutionAuthority.reconcile` to `Executor.reconcile` found that the legacy state-mutating path accepted the first RPC transaction response, did not compare its signature to the pending order, and used a separate permissive delta parser. (2) Current Solana `getTransaction` documentation defines a nullable response with nullable `meta`, transaction `slot`/`version`, and a finalized commitment option; the shared response includes error, fee, account-indexed lamport balances, token balances, and signatures. The implementation must preserve missing/unknown outcomes rather than coerce them. [Solana getTransaction](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/gettransaction), [Solana RPC JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures). (3) Jito documents inflight `Failed` as a bundle not forwarded and `Landed` as bundle-level landing evidence, while also documenting uncles/rebroadcast behavior; neither status binds the returned transaction effects to SYLPH's pending signed intent. Relay status therefore cannot directly settle local wallet economics. [Jito bundle status and failure guidance](https://docs.jito.wtf/lowlatencytxnsend/).

**Adversarial challenge.** A stale, inconsistent, or wrong-signature RPC response could previously be labeled filled and mutate the local ledger. A Jito drop status could previously be labeled failed before checking whether the signature appeared in finalized chain history. A provider could also return token metadata with malformed quantities or lamport arrays that did not conserve. These are accounting-truth defects even while new live execution remains blocked.

**Changes and five-upgrade register.** (1) **P0, implemented:** exact instruction-bound tip and wallet-paid associated-account funding from cycle 126; proven funding is removed from trade-only SOL delta, unresolved funding remains visible. (2) **P0, implemented:** `transactionDeltas` now delegates to `AssetDeltaEngine`, demands successful explicit metadata, complete safe balances, canonical raw token amounts, and global lamport conservation, while returning gross wallet SOL movement for the ledger. (3) **P0, implemented:** the legacy reconciler now requires every configured RPC to return the same finalized signature, slot, error/fee, and canonical economic-balance snapshot before reporting filled/failed; one found result against a null/error response remains pending. (4) **P0, implemented:** Jito failure is now logged as a diagnostic and chain evidence still decides the outcome. The reconciler only releases expiry after every endpoint reports a finalized height past the existing margin. (5) **P1, open:** endpoint URLs are not proof of independent infrastructure; add provider-family attestations and a durable, message-bound settlement receipt before this path can become certified settlement authority.

No RuntimeRoot schema/API widening was made. A compatibility audit of current code/tests confirms schema-v2 roots hash the complete 256-bit feature-set digest, reject coerced epoch/context/feature values, preserve legacy v1's 16-hex preimage, and retain transaction support default `all`; the full platform suite includes `runtime-root-hash-schema.test.mjs`. This check is not an independent reviewer certification. Endpoint agreement is only agreement among configured RPC URLs and does not prove independent operators or authenticated truth.

**Capturable multiplier impact.** These changes do not predict earlier winners. They prevent false settled outcomes and fee/cost misclassification from contaminating realized PnL, capture efficiency, and future calibration. That is a prerequisite for measuring whether a candidate was executable and capturable. No strategy, allocation, threshold, dependency, schema, signer, or submission authority changed.

## Research cycle 128 — enrich finalized signature observations with message/account effects — 2026-10-07

**Repository and history context.** Continued from cycle 127 and inspected the finalized block auditor, the quarantined certified coordinator, the legacy `Store`/SQLite worker contracts, the CPI simulation certificate proposal, and the prior “Upgrade sylph-fusion with Sol” and related Solana evidence work. The existing block auditor is the right observation boundary; duplicating it as a second receipt/certificate format would create competing semantics. Its provider-specific targeted CPI trace already resolves v0 lookup-table account indexes, but the previous result omitted the exact decoded message identity and raw account balance context. The generic audit-event API is append-only and deduplicated but is not a typed, validated settlement-evidence store. No storage schema/API was widened.

**Three findings.** (1) Solana `getBlock` full transaction metadata exposes `loadedAddresses`, pre/post lamport arrays, token-balance rows, and transaction metadata as distinct nullable/optional values; absence cannot safely mean zero. [Solana RPC JSON structures](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures), [getBlock](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getblock). (2) The auditor's v0 account index space is ordered static keys, loaded writable, loaded readonly; targeted evidence binds that exact reported order and the serialized decoded message. The reported first Ed25519 signature is now verified against the first required signer and decoded message using the project's existing `transaction-artifact.ts` crypto pattern; this proves pair consistency, not that the RPC truthfully returned block membership, finality, or metadata. (3) Lamport arrays are JavaScript numeric RPC values while Solana balances are u64; values outside the exactly-safe integer range must not be stringified into apparently exact deltas. The new evidence accepts only nonnegative safe integers and exact account-count alignment, labels absent data `UNAVAILABLE` and unsafe/misaligned data `MALFORMED`, and never derives a wallet delta here.

**Implemented changes.** Added bounded signature-targeted evidence fields to `FinalizedTransactionCpiEvidence`: decoded message SHA-256, first-signature Ed25519 verification status (`VERIFIED`, `MISMATCH`, `UNAVAILABLE`), ordered static/loaded keys, safe raw pre/post lamport balance arrays, a distinct availability state, and a digest over the reported pre/post token rows (preserving missing vs explicit values). Existing trace hashes now cover these fields. Added deterministic signed-message and mismatched-signature tests, as well as ALT ordering, aligned balances, absent balance arrays, malformed alignment, and explicit non-authority cases. Updated the integration inventory with current limits. A repository-wide import search shows no production consumer of this auditor; the `RpcPool` adapter exposes it for read-only diagnostics only.

**Adversarial boundary.** Signature verification proves the provider-reported signature matches the provider-decoded message and first signer; it does not show that this message matches the locally authorized signed intent or authenticate the RPC's block membership, finality, and metadata assertions. Agreement among configured endpoints does not establish independent operators. Token rows are committed as reported, not converted into owner/mint deltas by this observer. Missing/invalid balances cannot be treated as zero. Provider labels and configured URLs are not provenance attestations. There is still no durable receipt, replay key, retention contract, or crash/restart verification for this evidence. The coordinator's `reconcileFinalChainOutcome` remains quarantined, and live signing/submission remain unavailable.

**Verification and system state.** `npm run build:engine` passed; focused finalized-block audit tests passed 20/20. Full `npm test` exited 0: core 413, intelligence 561, platform 903, terminal 306 (2,183 total). `git diff --check` passed for tracked documentation; Git emitted only the existing large-worktree CRLF normalization notices. The local service was not restarted and no Solana endpoint was contacted. The worktree still contains 157 modified/untracked paths from ongoing project work; unrelated changes were preserved.

**Next highest-value work.** Independently review compatibility and evidence semantics before persistence. Then capture the exact signed wire/message hash from the durable signing journal and require it to match this verified decoded message; the present observer proves the RPC-reported signature signs its decoded message but is not yet joined to the locally authorized signed intent. Design a separate immutable SQLite receipt schema with canonical revalidation on reads, stable replay identity, bounded retention, and process-death tests, and bind provider family/config revisions plus finalized slot/outcome/effects. Only after those gates should receipt evidence be evaluated by a separately reviewed reconciliation authority. Continue to keep strategy labels and the supplied peak/drop CSV research-only until landed outcomes are trustworthy.

## Research cycle 129 — expose and compare persisted signed-intent identity — 2026-10-07

**Repository/history review.** Re-read the current finalized block auditor, `RpcPool.auditFinalizedBlockRange`, `transaction-artifact.ts`, `DurableLiveSigner`, `Store`, the SQLite generation schema/worker, and the quarantined certified coordinator. Rechecked current public GitHub discovery, but GitHub's REST URLs were not accessible to the browser tool; the last successful repository API check remains the cycle-127 snapshot, so remote branch/issue/PR state is not newly verified in this cycle. Existing project-chat analysis, including the “Solana Simulation Evidence” work and Sol/upgrades threads, keeps simulation traces separate from landed RPC observations and emphasizes that byte hashes alone are not authenticity. The code's signing journal stores `wallet`, `message_sha256`, signed state, and signature bytes, but exposed no read method.

**Three research findings.** (1) Solana specifies each required transaction signer signature as Ed25519 over the exact serialized message; signatures authorize bytes, so fee payer, blockhash, account list, instruction order, or instruction data changes invalidate the signature. [Solana partial signing](https://solana.com/docs/core/transactions/partial-signing), [transaction pipeline verification](https://solana.com/docs/core/transactions/transaction-pipeline). (2) Agave indexes signatures by required signer order, making the first transaction signature correspond to the first required signer; the prior auditor now verifies that first signature against the first signer. [Solana transaction pipeline](https://solana.com/docs/core/transactions/transaction-pipeline). (3) Node 24's `crypto.verify` requires a null algorithm for Ed25519 and returns boolean signature validity; this matches both the existing local `transaction-artifact.ts` path and the targeted finalized auditor. [Node.js v24 crypto](https://nodejs.org/download/release/v24.16.0/docs/api/crypto.html).

**Implemented changes.** Added a parameterized, read-only `Store.getSigningIntent(economicIntentId)` query returning only intent ID, wallet, message hash, state, and signature; public/worker input bounds and read-time canonical hash/base64 checks fail closed. It excludes generation-registration rows and does not change the SQLite schema or signing writes. Added process-reopen coverage for PREPARED/SIGNED records. Added `correlateFinalizedTransactionToSigningIntent`, a pure diagnostic comparator checking the locally stored message hash, base64 signature, first signer wallet, observed base58 signature, and the auditor's Ed25519 status. Its result names explicitly say `REPORTED`; it cannot authenticate caller-supplied evidence, certify settlement, release a reservation, or be used as proof without a future private receipt boundary. The certified coordinator and legacy reconciliation paths do not consume it.

**Adversarial limits.** If the local signing record is still PREPARED after a crash, correlation remains unavailable because signer completion is ambiguous. If the wallet is not the first required signer (for example, a multi-signer format outside the current one-signer artifact contract), the comparator remains unavailable. A field comparator can be forged by fabricating its arguments; no cryptographic receipt or opaque in-process observer capability is issued. The signing journal read API is additive and schema-neutral, but its compatibility deserves independent review before any authority integration. Provider family, operator independence, block inclusion proof, and finalized metadata authenticity remain open.

**Verification.** `npm run build:engine` passed. Focused finalized-auditor and generation-signing-store suites passed 110/110 (20 auditor, 90 storage). The storage regression confirms missing-row behavior, PREPARED/SIGNED states, generation-registration exclusion, invalid lookup rejection, frozen results, and exact signature/hash recovery after closing and reopening SQLite. The comparator test covers exact match, message-hash disagreement, wallet mismatch/unsupported signer ordering, and PREPARED state. No signing, submission, Solana RPC, schema migration, commit, or push occurred. Full suite still needs to be rerun after this API addition; the paper runtime remains untouched.

**Next highest-value action.** Run the complete suite and obtain independent compatibility review of `Store.getSigningIntent` and the diagnostic comparator. Then create a private receipt boundary that can only correlate direct auditor output with the journal row, persist canonical receipt bytes under a separate schema with replay identity, and test process death before/after receipt commit. Keep terminal state changes quarantined until provider provenance and the durable receipt are independently reviewed.
## Research cycle 130 — restore paper terminal reachability in Docker

**Branch gate.** GitHub now exposes `feat/certification-layer-c1-c4` at the frozen baseline `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`; `main` remains at that SHA. The checkout is on the authorized feature branch. Existing dirty work was retained.

**Finding and change.** Docker publishes host `127.0.0.1:8793` to container port 8793, but the terminal bound to container loopback, so port forwarding could not reach the listener. Docker's networking documentation distinguishes the host publication address from the container listener and documents host networking as Linux-only or an opt-in Docker Desktop feature; host networking would not be a portable default. The terminal now uses an explicit Compose-only `SYLPH_DOCKER_LOOPBACK_PUBLISHED=true` mode that binds the container listener to its interfaces only while `MODE=paper` and Docker's `/.dockerenv` marker is present. The request guard permits Docker's forwarded peer only in that mode and still checks loopback Host/Origin authorities. Non-container startup remains loopback-bound. Compose keeps the published host IP at 127.0.0.1. See [Docker published ports](https://docs.docker.com/get-started/docker-concepts/running-containers/publishing-ports/) and [Docker host networking limitations](https://docs.docker.com/engine/network/drivers/host/).

**Verification.** Focused network-boundary tests passed 12/12; the complete terminal suite passed 309/309; `docker compose config --quiet`, `node --check` for the server and new binding module, and `git diff --check` passed. The local Docker client is version 29.8.0, but its Linux engine pipe is unavailable, so container startup, forwarded HTTP reachability, and external-client rejection remain unverified. No service was restarted and no transaction or Solana RPC was issued.

**Remaining.** Start Docker Desktop and verify `http://127.0.0.1:8793/health` through the published port, reject requests to non-loopback Host/Origin values and confirm the port is not published on LAN interfaces. Then continue certification Step 1. This change is uncommitted and no release/certification claim is made.

**Context.** Continued from the attached System Integration Certification Report and its C5 gate. The local `feat/certification-layer-c1-c4` branch tracks `origin/feat/certification-layer-c1-c4` at `6e1d268a5bf22e957832b5fabd92ea1f0ab0c67c`; `main` tracks `origin/main` at `373143a10d05327149cc5d3cbd0cfb44bff9cc18`. GitHub currently shows zero open issues and zero open or closed pull requests; its public README describes an implementation candidate whose components are not all composed into one production path ([repository](https://github.com/MrPineapples22/sylph-fusion), [issues](https://github.com/MrPineapples22/sylph-fusion/issues), [pull requests](https://github.com/MrPineapples22/sylph-fusion/pulls)). The report was cross-checked against the actual checkout, the “Upgrade sylph-fusion with Sol” and “SOL-SYLPH” project chats, earlier profit-review conversations, and saved Solana Simulation Evidence/CPI research. Those sources support stronger evidence boundaries and do not justify increasing live execution authority. The attached report was treated as a claim to verify, not as proof.

**Durable ingress recovery.** The working branch now has a `canonical_ingress_delivery_v1` SQLite outbox. The worker writes the canonical ingress audit row and delivery row within the same transaction, persists acknowledgement after subscribers complete, retains unacknowledged rows during pruning, and keeps stable dedupe identity after delivered-row pruning. Recovery validates the stored canonical event and payload, re-runs the current compiler and validator, and redispatches pending work. The crash test now covers a failed subscriber, database reopen, prune-before-replay, successful retry, durable acknowledgement, and prune-after-delivery. The normal test-only in-memory journal now acknowledges in-process delivery; a shared Feed fixture supplies an explicit no-op test subscriber so these tests still exercise delivery acceptance.

This follows the outbox pattern's stable event identity and consumer deduplication guidance ([Debezium Outbox Event Router](https://debezium.io/documentation/reference/stable/transformations/outbox-event-router.html)) and SQLite's requirement to keep WAL state with the database and use commit synchronization for durability ([SQLite Write-Ahead Logging](https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html)). The outbox provides **at-least-once** dispatch. A process can die after applying a downstream effect and before acknowledgement, so exactly-once effects remain unproven until reducer/candidate consumers use durable idempotency or an atomic effect-plus-checkpoint protocol.

**C5 evidence hardening.** The previous C5 certify path accepted arbitrary caller-supplied objects labeled `REAL_RUNTIME`; placeholder spans were enough to receive C5. Certify mode now ignores those objects and reads only the fixed persisted artifact. The artifact schema requires runtime/process identity, bounded span fields, a claimed `FSYNC_COMMITTED` receipt, and a canonical SHA-256. Review showed that a schema and hash still do not authenticate who produced the artifact, so the verifier now also requires an Ed25519 signature over the evidence payload and a configured pinned public key. `config/certification-config.json` currently has no trust root, and there is no connected runtime telemetry producer or artifact; therefore no `REAL_RUNTIME` evidence is accepted and C5 remains unawarded. A later producer must attest actual process/source identity and a durable event that the verifier can independently look up in SQLite. This is consistent with OpenTelemetry's trace context and resource identity being correlation metadata that still needs a trustworthy source ([OpenTelemetry JavaScript](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/js/), [Propagation](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/js/propagation/), [Resources](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/js/resources/)).

**Certification discrepancy found.** `npm run audit:c1` now rebuilds current TypeScript, runs the negative compilation and physical runtime checks, records all stopping metrics at zero, and reports C1–C4 PASS. The separate static connectivity compiler reports C1: its input has no source-bound test-run receipt, and its graph from `src/fusion.ts` reaches only two of four configured authority modules. `npm run audit:convergence:certify` consequently halts at `C4_GATE_HALT` before inspecting C5. These are different evidence contracts; the physical auditor's result does not satisfy or override the static convergence gate. The unreachable decision and terminality authorities need runtime-composition tracing and truthful graph edges, while the receipt generator must bind current source inventory, exact test command/set, runtime version, and exit result. Do not add unused imports or a caller-controlled “tests passed” flag to force C4.

**Verification.** `npm run build:engine` passed after the outbox fixture correction. Targeted Feed, crash-recovery, C5, and certification negative-control tests passed; the latest repository-wide `npm run test:all` passed core 531/531, intelligence 561/561, platform 909/909, and terminal 309/309 (2,310 total). A final `npm run audit:c1` on HEAD `2ecb9b2` reported C1/C2/C3/C4 PASS, all stopping metrics zero, runtime tests passing, and evidence manifest SHA-256 `63e7aaf02a58173e8fc8dae986bc5cd1c0269f0beb8ead979823328aed801e72`. `npm run audit:connectivity:static` completed with 668 TypeScript modules and zero compile diagnostics, but reported highest level C1. `npm run audit:convergence:certify` stopped at C1/C4_GATE_HALT. No runtime process was started, no Solana RPC was contacted, and no signing, deployment, or live trading action occurred.

**Open work.** Independently review the outbox transaction and retry semantics; add downstream idempotency/checkpointing and crash-after-effect-before-ack tests; implement a real telemetry producer plus independent durable-record verification; provision its attestation trust root through reviewed deployment configuration; generate a source-bound test-run receipt; and resolve the two unreachable authority paths based on actual composition. C5–C10 and unified certification remain unproven, and live capital remains blocked. Research cycle 133's C1 delivery failure is historical and superseded by the recovery result here; its authenticity and exactly-once caveats remain open.

## Research cycle 135 — legacy outbox migration uncertainty — 2026-10-07

**Input and review.** Read the user-provided System Integration Certification Report for branch `feat/certification-layer-c1-c4`, which claims physical C1–C4 PASS and correctly caps static certification at C4. Independent review confirmed that current-format append/ack transactions and fresh-install crash recovery are atomic, while noting that an upgrade from a pre-outbox database cannot infer whether an old in-memory callback completed. The report's evaluated HEAD (`9aeb9cf`) differs from this checkout (`6e1d268`) and its evidence is a snapshot, not proof for the current worktree.

**Change.** `ensureIngressDeliverySchema()` now migrates pre-outbox committed records into an explicit `LEGACY_UNKNOWN` delivery state. It records a one-time durable `canonical_ingress_legacy_delivery_migration_v1` audit marker with row count and a digest of observation IDs, preserves dedupe history, and excludes those records from automatic live replay. The migration refuses to invent a successful-delivery timestamp. Older outbox schemas with an acknowledgement timestamp are upgraded to explicit `ACKNOWLEDGED` or `PENDING` status. New commits remain `PENDING` until durable acknowledgement; legacy unknown records cannot be acknowledged by the ordinary replay path and require a future explicit rebuild procedure.

**Verification.** `npm run build:engine` passed. The targeted canonical-ingress durability and crash-recovery suites passed 6/6, including a simulated pre-outbox database proving that a historical commit is not replayed and its uncertainty marker/dedupe evidence survive migration. The full suite and physical auditor have not yet been rerun against this source change; previously sealed C1–C4 evidence must not be read as certifying the modified source.

**Remaining high-value work.** The Engine still acknowledges delivery after its in-memory callback returns, while canonical state and candidate/trade projections are not restored from a durable checkpoint. A crash after callback completion can therefore lose Engine state, and retry before acknowledgement can duplicate non-idempotent effects. Implement a durable, idempotent Engine projection/checkpoint and test crashes on both sides of acknowledgement. Recovery also pins compiler and validator versions to current code; support a reviewed version registry or a controlled, fail-closed migration for pending events across deployments. C5–C10 remain unproven; live capital remains blocked.

## Research cycle 136 — atomic legacy migration and final platform verification — 2026-10-07

**Additional safety finding.** A migration that creates the outbox table and classifies old rows in separate transactions can strand a database if the process dies between those steps: the next startup sees the table and may assume migration is complete. The schema creation, old-row classification, and uncertainty marker are now enclosed in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (or the caller's existing transaction). Empty new databases do not get a spurious legacy-migration audit record. Legacy rows carry a distinct `LEGACY_UNKNOWN` status with a null acknowledgement timestamp; ordinary acknowledgement rejects them.

**Verification.** Added a malformed-legacy-row test proving failed classification rolls back schema creation atomically. Final `npm run build:engine` and the focused ingress durability/crash-recovery tests passed (7/7). The final full platform suite passed 911/911. `npm run test:all` passed on the immediately preceding implementation (before the final transaction wrapper and rollback test); it is not claimed as a run on the final byte-for-byte tree. The final `npm run audit:c1` passed C1–C4, all physical stopping metrics were zero, and physical runtime checks passed. Its manifest records base commit `6401c16ebc849ad1450d08f8dda15a69aa09396a` and explicitly lists the modified working-tree files, so this is evidence for a dirty working tree and must not be represented as a clean committed release certificate. `git diff --check` passed.

**Outstanding integration risks.** Engine downstream effects still lack durable projection/checkpoint semantics, and pending replay still fails closed on compiler or validator drift. The separate static connectivity compiler/C0–C10 convergence gate remains at C1 due to missing source-bound test receipt and two unreachable authority paths. C5 has no connected signed runtime telemetry producer or provisioned trust root. Do not award unified certification or enable live capital.

Settlement had also generated two local `FINALIZED` witnesses from a single caller timestamp; it now issues an `UNKNOWN` verdict with an empty witness list unless independently supplied terminality evidence already exists, and the learning-ready flag is gated on authoritative terminality.

## Research cycle 138 — source-bound full-suite receipt advances static gate to C4 — 2026-10-07

**Finding.** The prior C2 gate accepted a caller-supplied boolean and even a `--test-run-passed` CLI flag. That let a user assertion advance the static score despite the compiler correctly stating that test references were not evidence. Removed the override. C2 now requires the receipt verifier's structured PASS result.

**Implementation and research.** Added `npm run test:certified`, which runs the exact `npm run test:all` command, streams its output while incrementally hashing it, and writes a PASS/FAIL receipt only after process exit. The receipt binds the 668-file TypeScript source manifest/root, source sizes and SHA-256 values, `package.json`, lockfile, `tsconfig`, exact command, test output digest, exit code, start/end timestamps, Node/npm versions, platform, and architecture. The compiler recomputes the source/configuration identities and receipt hash before accepting C2. This adopts SLSA 1.2's provenance direction—identify the build invocation, materials, and producing environment, and distribute immutable attestations—while deliberately documenting that this local hash receipt lacks a trusted builder signature or external transparency record ([SLSA provenance](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-provenance), [distribution guidance](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/distributing-provenance)). Tests prove a legacy boolean cannot substitute, nonzero/tampered/malformed receipts fail closed, and a stale or wrong source root does not verify. Compiler regression tests explicitly use a missing receipt path, so a prior valid local receipt cannot make those tests order-dependent.

**Verification.** Targeted compiler and receipt tests passed 16/16 before the final runtime-field refinement; the final receipt/static-core tests passed 8/8. The final `npm run test:certified` run passed Core 536/536, Intelligence 562/562, Platform 913/913, and Terminal 309/309 (2,320 tests); it wrote receipt `19a18d0203693fd4b5a4a790d3a355e22b77580ef0c6d4f6c9e1de19d92795c3` for source root `215b790130a989b43514f27aee63cf0ed60a97ed4cfd37da98ef20f436bfd381` on Node 24.21.0/npm 11.19.0. `npm run audit:connectivity:static` then verified that exact receipt and reached C4 with all four configured authority paths reachable. The scorecard still holds C5–C10 false and explicitly labels the receipt as local integrity evidence, not a signed or independently trusted execution attestation.

**Integrity limits and next work.** A local actor who can rewrite both the receipt and generated artifacts can create a matching digest; this receipt closes stale/mistaken evidence gaps, not hostile-host forgery. Add a CI or supervisor signature/trust root and preserve the full test log as a durable artifact before treating the test result as independently attested. C5 still lacks a connected signed runtime telemetry producer and provisioned trust root. Unified certification remains false and live capital stays blocked.
