## Research cycle 141 — connect signed runtime telemetry to canonical ingress and bounded shutdown export — 2026-10-07

**Report/history alignment.** The supplied System Integration Certification Report describes C1–C4 as passed, C4 as the static/ancestry ceiling, and live capital as blocked. The current branch's physical audit and certify-mode convergence reproduce that boundary. Prior Sol/Sylph work on stream continuity, paper-only exits, and simulation evidence supports an evidence-first approach; simulation/test fixtures do not prove production runtime behavior.

**Implementation.** Added opt-in `RuntimeTelemetryCapture` bound to the exact Store-backed ingress capability. It records actual compile, validate, durable commit, downstream delivery, and retry operations as bounded V2 spans. Each attempt has a nonzero root trace; child spans and retry roots carry explicit, verifier-checked ancestry and status. Capture persists the immutable span batch under a content-derived stable ID before signing, rereads and hashes the exact SQLite row, binds it to a durable logical Store instance identity, and checks a separate injected Ed25519 signer against the configured public-key pin before returning evidence. V1 verification remains supported.

`runEngine` accepts explicit host-supplied signer, public pin, source build identity, and export path. The periodic exporter serializes bounded captures, writes with restrictive permissions and atomic rename, and runs its bounded final drain before Store cleanup. The default CLI remains disabled without explicit host injection; no transaction wallet is reused and no key is generated or provisioned. A stalled signer or publication is fenced; unresolved rename at shutdown is surfaced as `C5_PUBLICATION_PENDING_AT_SHUTDOWN` while remaining resources, including Store, are closed.

**Independent review and verification.** Three independent review passes checked Store binding/signature compatibility and then runtime composition, trace lineage, timeout behavior, and shutdown publication. Findings were fixed: delivery now shares truthful trace lineage, rejected ingress roots are `ERROR`, the verifier rejects invalid ancestry, and shutdown reports unresolved publication instead of implying success. `npm run build:engine` passed. The final certified run passed Core 537, Intelligence 562, Platform 923, and Terminal 309 tests (2,331 total) and wrote receipt `d50c2af05b71094d74d61fec435d4a4751adfa7dc6a04f50e842c0538cb27925`, binding 1,257 execution files to inventory root `b5c13694fd31e6e66ac9883a2dfbdce2cb80957e4e0174cd5149bf983a8c14e2`, TypeScript root `6795d36a38b98d770ca156d9a28b6335b7ddacc9b29c43d4a2a22538b8bb0e88`, and Node 24.21.0/npm 11.19.0. Focused producer tests passed 10/10 after final fixes. Static connectivity and the physical C1–C4 audit passed with all stopping metrics zero. The integration certificate independently verified at C4.

**Remaining limits and next work.** Certify-mode convergence still stops at C4 with `C5_DURABLE_RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_MISSING`: no production attestation key, trust configuration, or real runtime artifact has been provisioned. Host-supplied source commit/tree values are assertions until bound to an independently verified release identity. A copied SQLite file preserves its logical Store UUID, and recognized local filesystem type cannot prove backing hardware durability. C6–C10, unified certification, and live capital remain blocked. Next work should define trusted release-identity injection and an operator-managed attestation service/pin lifecycle, then verify an actual paper-runtime artifact against a separately provisioned trust root before reevaluating C5.

## Research cycle 142 — dedicated runtime-attestation KMS cryptographic adapter — 2026-10-07

**Boundary and research.** The cycle-141 runtime capture accepts a dedicated signer but has no concrete KMS implementation. Reusing the transaction `AwsKmsEd25519` signer would blur wallet and attestation authority. Added `AwsKmsRuntimeAttestationSigner`, implementing only `RuntimeAttestationSigner`, with a type-only dependency on the existing transport contract. AWS's official [Sign API](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/APIReference/API_Sign.html) requires `RAW` with `ED25519_SHA_512`; its [GetPublicKey API](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/APIReference/API_GetPublicKey.html) supplies public-key metadata and bytes for external verification. The existing runtime evidence protocol signs the 32-byte SHA-256 root as the pure Ed25519 message.

**Implementation.** Connection requires a concrete UUID key ARN and an explicit public-only Ed25519 PEM pin. It checks the exact returned key ARN, specification, usage, signing algorithm, and canonical DER/SPKI public bytes against that pin. Signing accepts a canonical lowercase root, copies exactly its 32 bytes into the RAW request, checks response identity/algorithm and signature length, verifies an independent signature snapshot locally, and returns canonical base64. Private fields retain the transport/key/pin; no wallet or transaction-signing API is exposed. `docs/runtime-attestation-kms.md` defines host composition and the operational limits.

**Author verification; independent review pending.** `npm run build:engine` passed. The focused adapter, existing transaction KMS, and runtime telemetry producer run passed 24/24 tests (10 new adapter tests, four existing transaction tests, 10 producer tests). New cases reject aliases and malformed ARNs, invalid/private/non-Ed25519 pins, wrong or malformed KMS metadata, unpinned/noncanonical DER, invalid roots, UTF-8 or double-hash signing, mutable request bytes, response key/algorithm/type/length/signature corruption, and transport denial. They prove stable verification/evidence despite later mutation of public-key and signature buffers. These are mock-only tests. No full-suite receipt or physical certificate has yet been regenerated for this source change, and the previous source-bound receipt is now stale. Critical authoring is complete and awaits the required independent Astra review before acceptance.

**Unresolved deployment requirements.** No AWS SDK adapter, credentials, KMS account actions, keys, permissions, or trust configuration were added. A host must supply an isolated transport and a separately provisioned runtime key ARN/pin; the wrapper cannot prove IAM isolation or enforce operational separation from a wallet key by itself. It signs a claimed root, so trusted attestation service authorization and independently verified release/runtime identity remain necessary. UUID-style key IDs are supported; multi-region `mrk-` identifiers are not. Actual AWS interoperability, production signer availability, and real paper-runtime evidence remain unverified. C5–C10 and live capital remain blocked.

## Research cycle 143 — enforce runtime-only KMS adapter construction — 2026-10-07

**Independent-review finding and repair.** Astra review found that TypeScript's `private constructor` is emitted as a public JavaScript constructor. A direct JS caller could therefore bypass `connect`'s concrete-ARN, KMS metadata, and public-pin checks. Added a module-private unique-symbol construction seal; the constructor rejects any value other than the inaccessible seal before storing transport or key capabilities. The connection factory supplies it only after successful KMS metadata and SPKI pin validation. A regression test executes the generated JavaScript and covers the original alias/private-key call and forged local/global symbols, with zero transport calls, while confirming normal validated connection still signs.

**Independent review and verification.** The independent Astra reviewer reproduced the original bypass, withheld approval, then reviewed the repair and approved it. The emitted-JavaScript guard was inspected directly. `npm run build:engine` passed; focused adapter/transaction KMS/runtime telemetry producer checks passed 25/25 (11 adapter, four transaction KMS, 10 producer). The current `npm run test:certified` passed Core 537/537, Intelligence 562/562, Platform 934/934, and Terminal 309/309 (2,342 total). The final source-bound receipt is `fa496517220bff01717a58762d0bba23305de7c8e501451b76ce0df755691dad`; it binds 1,259 execution files to inventory root `15f84d8c7b66f56a6a2eac2cb43f4d54acb2d330d9ace0bfefbb025ce851a3aa` on Node 24.21.0/npm 11.19.0. Static connectivity passed with C4 ceiling enforced. The physical audit passed C1–C4 with every stopping metric zero; its latest recorded digest is regenerated after this ledger update. Certify-mode convergence still stops at C4 with `C5_DURABLE_RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_MISSING`; the integration certificate verifies at C4. `git diff --check` passed with only the repository's Windows line-ending notices.

**Remaining limits.** This review approves the code contract only. Tests use ephemeral keys and mock transports. AWS interoperability, IAM isolation, an independently verified release identity, provisioned trust pin/key, and a real paper-runtime artifact remain unverified. C5–C10, unified certification, and live capital remain blocked.

## Research cycle 144 — attest the actual Windows candidate without granting runtime authority — 2026-10-07

**Finding and research.** The existing Windows packager inventories runtime bytes but CI neither preserves that package nor signs its identity. Added a separate manual, canonical-repository/main-only candidate workflow using the existing source build and offline suites. It packages the tested runtime as a tar.gz, records the checked-out commit/tree and archive digest, attests both archive and identity sidecar with a pinned `actions/attest`, and uploads the subjects and bundle. The official [GitHub CLI verifier](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_attestation_verify) supports exact source/signer commit, workflow certificate identity, issuer, ref, repository, predicate and hosted-runner constraints. It also explicitly warns that predicate fields are controlled by the workflow. [SLSA verification guidance](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/verifying-artifacts) distinguishes verified signatures/subjects from trusted builder policy; this pipeline does not claim an isolated builder or SLSA Build L3.

**Implementation and authority boundary.** `scripts/prepare-ci-candidate.mjs` requires the expected workflow context, rejects source edits/untracked files and mismatched checkout SHA, preserves previous outputs, and delegates runtime closure/inventory to the existing packager. `scripts/candidate-provenance.mjs` requires independently supplied commit/tree policy, invokes the real GitHub CLI separately on both signed subjects, verifies subject/digest/schema binding, and checks inputs have not changed. It has no caller-supplied verifier or success boolean. Its informational receipt explicitly denies runtime authority and certification and states that extracted runtime bytes are unverified. There is no connection to BuildSeal or C5, and telemetry source fields remain host assertions. `docs/candidate-provenance.md` records policy, usage, trusted-host assumptions, archive/runtime/dependency measurement gaps, and maintainer steps.

**Author verification; independent review pending.** The candidate verifier suite passed 8/8, covering strict policy/schema, both verification calls, subprocess failure, malformed/missing subjects, failed sidecar signature, concurrent file changes, invalid CI contexts, and a real temporary Git checkout packaged through `tar` with source and archive checks. The existing package-candidate suite passed 5/5. Process stubs exercise orchestration only and do not prove Sigstore cryptography. `npm run build:engine` was attempted but failed on a concurrent change outside this cycle: `src/platform/ingestion/yellowstone-truth-bridge.ts:193` supplies `wireEncoding` to a type that does not define it (TS2353). No real hosted workflow or GitHub CLI verification was run; `gh` is unavailable in this local shell. The new scripts/tests invalidate the previous certified receipt. Independent Astra review and regenerated certification evidence are required before acceptance.

**Remaining requirements.** A maintainer must review/land the workflow on main, confirm attestation availability/policy, dispatch it, preserve the bundle and both subjects before artifact expiry, and verify them with a trusted CLI against independently approved commit/tree IDs. The signer commit pin requires review of that exact workflow revision; a workflow-controlled tree claim is not independent builder isolation. The verifier does not extract/measure the launched program or installed dependencies, protect against a hostile local OS, or provision the runtime signer/trust pin. No deployment, release, dispatch, commit, push, key action, C5–C10 promotion, or live-capital action was performed by this authoring cycle.

## Research cycle 149 — reject stale certification manifests — 2026-10-08

**Finding.** The submitted System Integration Certification Report described commit `9aeb9cf`; during this audit, the feature branch advanced through `f1e0b54`, `95dad96`, and `8d314748cd8ad1b19b54613a1eacef884c80e9f9`. Fresh C1–C4 artifacts passed, but certificate generation consumed a checked-in manifest still bound to `f1e0b54` and the placeholder mandatory-edge digest `eeee…`. The certificate verifier proved only that the new certificate matched its own payload; it did not detect stale input provenance. A second integration gap was that the certificate did not bind the physical C1–C4 audit bundle.

**Repair.** Added `verifyCertificationManifest` to validate the manifest root, exact checkout SHA, current lockfile and TypeScript config digests, certification config digest, real mandatory-edge-set digest, and configured edge count. Normal certificate generation now invokes this validation before assembling a persisted certificate. Manifest generation now binds the actual checkout SHA and fails if a valid CI `GITHUB_SHA` disagrees with it. Synthetic injected manifests remain available only through explicit dry-run test calls. The integration certificate now also validates, checks out and hashes the common C1–C4 physical audit bundle, so its root directly binds those gate results. Dry-run checks retain root, commit, tree, and gate validation but skip live working-tree file checks; persisted generation performs the complete check.

**Verification.** The C1–C4 physical audit passed with all bypass and guard-failure metrics at zero. The full certified suite passed on the final source snapshot with receipt `63a5dc27a4d8b977197d038c5afd35b10f807ac3f2da90a6f85ca3142959e9b0`; the isolated manifest/certificate tests passed 19/19. Core passed 550 tests, Intelligence 562, Platform 953, and Terminal 309. Static connectivity and certify-mode convergence confirmed C4. The refreshed manifest binds checkout `8d314748cd8ad1b19b54613a1eacef884c80e9f9` and the actual 18-edge digest `14022f84fe8eca82bdbb8429c0be2549b5566dbeec5c5f2831340f76e6cf4e49`. The certificate now binds the physical C1–C4 audit root and passes independent verification. Convergence halts at C4 because signed C5 runtime evidence is absent. No C5–C10 evidence or live authority is asserted.

**Continuation audit.** Re-ran `npm run test:certification` on this worktree: 65/65 passed. `npm run audit:certificate:verify` passed at certificate root `2d87a6df081f15d4fb2037d31ba9072c94ee3fc969057d96bf7e89aa6fbf9c2c`. The physical report binds its three current uncommitted working-tree files by digest and records `workingTreeState`; therefore it is review evidence for this exact dirty checkout, not a clean committed release snapshot. No running Sylph runtime process was identified; unrelated Node processes were left untouched. `runtimeAttestationPublicKeyPem` remains null, and no `artifacts/connectivity/runtime-telemetry.json` exists. The runtime exporter is host-injected by `runEngine`, so there is no safe local source of a signed C5 artifact. To resume C5, an operator must approve/provision a dedicated Ed25519 trust root and isolated attestation signer, provide independently verified release commit/tree identity, launch the paper runtime with `runtimeTelemetry` exporter options and a controlled artifact path, preserve the corresponding SQLite DB, and run the existing evidence verifier/convergence certification against those exact inputs. Until then the measured ceiling remains C4; do not create a self-signed local key and present it as operator trust.

## Research cycle 151 — keep unsigned CPI observation separate from certificate lanes — 2026-10-08

**Finding.** The Solana simulation-evidence proposal had stale current-state text: it described an old `COMPLETE` trace status and said the observer did not validate stack paths. At HEAD `ddf1396500f7563d47255336d593c99a9d75b4fb`, `unsigned-observation-verifier.ts` already calls the reviewed bounded `cpi-trace-normalizer.ts`; the private receipt reports `STRUCTURALLY_VALID`, `PARTIAL`, or `UNAVAILABLE`, with node/group counts and trace hash. The normalized trace payload is discarded. `SimulationCertificate` remains strict at schemas 2.0/2.1, always records `UNAVAILABLE` in 2.1, and has no production caller.

**Compatibility review.** Astra author review ran the observer and frozen local legacy-fixture suites (75/75 passed) and left source unchanged. It found that the observer's request has `sigVerify: false` and `replaceRecentBlockhash: false`; the current certificate lanes encode different pairs (`SHADOW_SIMULATION` replaces the blockhash, while `EXACT_FINAL_SIMULATION` verifies a signature). Existing certificate inputs also include logs, invoked programs, balances, fees, and runtime/program roots that the opaque observer receipt does not contain. Combining those caller-supplied values with that receipt would not bind the resulting claims to the same RPC response. Keep the current schema and lanes unchanged until a design maps every response-derived field to one private receipt and represents the actual request flags. No provider authenticity or complete-CPI claim follows from an unkeyed digest.

**Protocol check and regression.** The current official [Solana `simulateTransaction` method](https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/simulatetransaction) defines `replaceRecentBlockhash` and `sigVerify` as independent options and says a recent blockhash is required unless replacement is enabled. The observer regression now asserts both current flag values as well as the CPI recording request, protecting the exact contract found in source. The existing observer, normalizer, and frozen certificate-fixture verification remains the evidence baseline; no certificate schema change is claimed.

**Next boundary.** Any future integration must choose an honest lane/schema for this unsigned, original-blockhash observer and either retain the exact receipt-bound response fields required by the certificate or define a separate narrow trace-observation artifact. Preserve frozen 2.0/2.1 behavior and clearly label serialized unkeyed hashes as self-consistency only. A separate Astra Protocol and Astra Verification review is required before adoption.

## Research cycle 150 — isolate certification test artifacts — 2026-10-08

**Finding.** The certification tests called the production static connectivity compiler and convergence evaluator with their default artifact paths. Negative-control fixtures consequently overwrote `c0-c10-scorecard.json` with a no-receipt C1 scorecard and `RUNTIME_CONVERGENCE_REPORT.json` with test-mode reports. A later certify run then consumed the test scorecard and stopped at C1, or disagreed with the saved certificate. The prior C4 architecture evidence was not disproved; the test harness had corrupted its generated inputs.

**Repair.** `runConnectivityCompiler` now accepts an optional `outputDir`, and `evaluateRuntimeConvergence` accepts an optional `outputPath`; production defaults remain unchanged. Connectivity and certification negative-control tests direct every generated report to unique OS temporary directories and clean those directories after each test file. Tests snapshot production artifact presence and digest when available, then assert the exact state remains unchanged; clean checkouts without generated artifacts remain supported. Research cycle 149 already added an isolated manifest test output path, so this closes the remaining certification-test artifact writes found by repository-wide call-site search.

**Independent review and verification.** Independent Astra review accepted the output routing and test cleanup. It found and prompted repair of a clean-checkout portability issue in the initial unchanged-artifact assertions; the final checks handle both present and absent generated artifacts. `npm run test:certification` passed 65/65, and before/after SHA-256 comparisons confirmed the production scorecard and convergence report remained byte-identical during the suite. The final source-bound `npm run test:certified` passed Core 550, Intelligence 562, Platform 953, and Terminal 309 (2,374 total), receipt `597d98cf7fc671f41d0e11113252dd6a1d7c02799529e768cf9c2d542dd2da21`. Static connectivity verified that receipt and reached C4; physical C1–C4 passed with all stopping metrics zero and runtime checks passing. The regenerated manifest binds HEAD `ddf1396500f7563d47255336d593c99a9d75b4fb` and the real 18-edge digest. Certify-mode convergence stops at C4 with `C5_DURABLE_RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_MISSING`; the regenerated C4 integration certificate passed independent verification. The source change passed independent review. These are local integrity and architecture audit results, not signed build provenance or a substitute for operator-provisioned C5 evidence.

**Next boundary.** C5 still requires an operator-approved Ed25519 trust root/signer and telemetry from the real paper runtime; no test fixture can satisfy that prerequisite. Keep C5–C10 and live authority blocked until those prerequisites are independently verified.

## Research cycle 152 — isolate certificate tests from saved certification state — 2026-10-08

**Pasted report reconciliation.** The supplied System Integration Certification Report describes commit `9aeb9cf` as evaluated on 2026-10-07. The current branch and remote feature ref are both `4151a7333361dc71f65725e3254dcf62c134176a`; the main ref remains `3e1053d04517a6aa24e376633884ccb56985e97e`. The report's 2,308-test total and C4 digest therefore are historical evidence and do not certify this checkout. Current checks reproduce C1–C4 PASS, zero reported physical stopping metrics, and a source-bound full-suite receipt. The static compiler now reaches all four configured authority modules, but its evidence is observed static imports and does not establish runtime use of every path. Earlier Feed/wire-capture limitations recorded in cycles 145–149 remain relevant; a zero metric is scoped to the physical auditor's implemented detectors and is not a universal proof that no bypass exists.

**Failure and repair.** The first full certified run found that the certificate-generator and negative-control tests loaded the persisted production certification manifest. That artifact was bound to prior commit `ddf1396500f7563d47255336d593c99a9d75b4fb`, so the tests failed correctly when the checkout was `4151a73`. The manifest freshness check was preserved. Certificate tests now pass explicit dry-run fixtures for the manifest, physical audit root, scorecard, authority/static graphs, and convergence report. The former “deterministic reproducibility” test only compared stable identity and level while the issuance timestamp changed; its title and comment now describe the behavior actually asserted. Production generation still verifies the current manifest and current physical audit.

**Verification.** The focused certificate/negative-control set passed 15/15 after fixture isolation. The final fresh `npm run test:certified` run passed Core 550, Intelligence 562, Platform 953, and Terminal 309 (2,374 total); receipt hash `110802a633e200a4968d31293ae8fb33c3166954d8829dcb71e60ad4b2303414`, source inventory root `c1383af882b51e152d62b5a677e83be416c94035d6280294ccc08d6e746a0a3d`. Static connectivity compiled 672 TypeScript modules with zero diagnostics, verified that receipt, and awarded C4. Physical audit on commit `4151a7333361dc71f65725e3254dcf62c134176a` reported C1–C4 PASS, all detector metrics zero, and runtime tests passing; evidence-manifest SHA-256 `a65e3b7a722eaf23a2a62d835870dc18ce00213bbbf743fad8e444225372ea72`. The regenerated manifest root is `b6cbcf05ff17890797bcde9a36b61e0ad19f9fc2c801b164ace655e6e9dc670c`. Certify-mode convergence halted at C4 with `C5_DURABLE_RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_MISSING`; the generated C4 integration certificate independently verified with root `9a3a0d828e9f4e8c07dcdb29d6c5ed4b2480511a3a2e23642d61b0d3d2629b81`.

**Research and next boundary.** Fresh review of the official OpenTelemetry docs confirms the Node resource detector can report PID, executable, command, and Node runtime version, while the process semantic conventions include creation-time identity fields; these are useful descriptors, not proof of loaded application bytes ([Node resources](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/js/resources/), [process semantic conventions](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/resource/process/)). The Propagators API describes carrying trace and baggage context across process boundaries, which supports correlation but does not provide an authenticity check ([Propagators API](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otel/context/api-propagators/)). SLSA v1.2 defines signed build provenance as an attestation from a trusted build platform about produced artifacts and build inputs; it can support artifact admission, but by itself it does not prove what a later running process loaded ([SLSA Build Provenance](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-provenance)). This matches the project gap recorded in `docs/candidate-provenance.md`: extraction/inventory/dependency/launch measurement is not connected to `RuntimeTelemetryOptions` or `BuildSealAuthority`. The implemented C5 verifier requires an Ed25519 signature and an exact durable SQLite audit row, but the checked-in trust root remains null and no real telemetry producer/artifact is connected. Next, specify and independently review a launch-to-receipt contract that authenticates the admitted build, binds that identity to the actual runtime process, and carries the same process/store/event identity into the signed durable receipt; include key custody/rotation and crash/restart behavior. Only then can an operator provision the trust root and run the real paper runtime. Do not synthesize C5 evidence from tests or from the pasted report.

## Research cycle 153 — bind C5 runtime receipts to current certified source and freshness — 2026-10-08

**Finding and external standard.** The signed runtime receipt already binds caller-supplied `sourceCommitSha` and `sourceTreeSha`, but the certify loader did not compare those claims with the checkout or its persisted C1–C4 physical audit. SQLite event binding prevented row substitution but did not prevent replay of a previously valid signed receipt. RFC 9334 §10 describes freshness as a verifier's local appraisal policy with an expiry threshold. It explains timestamp-based appraisal and the stronger nonce challenge approach, while noting nonce use adds a round trip and verifier state ([RFC 9334 §10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9334.html#section-10)).

**Implementation.** `scripts/runtime-telemetry-evidence.mjs` now verifies the persisted certification manifest against the current `HEAD`, derives `HEAD^{tree}` from Git, and invokes the tracked physical auditor in a fresh temporary output directory; mutable persisted C1–C4 projections are not read as authority. Certify mode similarly recomputes the static scorecard in a unique temporary directory and ignores the saved scorecard and caller-supplied baseline. The worktree must be clean except for the four ordinary unstaged physical-audit output files, which the auditor itself regenerates; other staged, unstaged, or untracked source changes fail closed. The signed telemetry commit/tree claims must exactly match. Capture end time must be no later than the verifier's local clock and at most five minutes old. The verifier samples its clock after source identity and physical audit resolution, then samples it again after the durable-store read immediately before accepting the receipt; lengthy verification cannot extend evidence freshness. Five minutes is a fixed policy constant based on the producer's default 250 ms cadence (configurable no higher than 60 s), so callers cannot widen the policy without changing reviewed code. Deterministic clock injection supports unit checks. `test/connectivity-runtime-convergence.test.mjs` covers a matching fixture, stale and future receipts, elapsed audit time, dirty checkout, source commit/tree mismatch, hidden Git index flags, and forged baseline inputs.

**Limits and next work.** The timestamp is an attested producer claim and relies on a trustworthy local verifier clock; five-minute expiry narrows replay exposure but does not prove process identity, loaded bytes, or continuous operation. RFC 9334's nonce approach would provide stronger challenge freshness but needs an online challenge/response protocol and replay-state lifecycle, which the current offline artifact loader does not have. A signed receipt still cannot prove loaded application bytes; connect independently verified build admission to process launch and runtime measurement before any C5 award. No trust root/key, runtime, or C5 claim was created. The source identity helper treats ignored files per Git status semantics; the configured telemetry artifact and SQLite database remain external runtime evidence paths and are not executed or trusted merely because they exist.

**Independent review repair.** The reviewer reproduced a bypass: Git status may omit modified tracked files with `assume-unchanged` or `skip-worktree` index flags. `verifyGitWorkingTreeAgainstHead` now rejects all non-normal tracked index tags, rejects staged/non-allowlisted changes, and hashes every tracked worktree file against its `HEAD` blob using Git's path-aware conversion. It rejects unsupported newline paths, symlinks/submodules, and custom `filter`, `working-tree-encoding`, or `ident` attributes rather than invoking filters or allowing a worktree conversion to normalize distinct bytes to the same blob. On POSIX it also compares the tracked executable mode with the actual file mode; Windows lacks reliable Unix executable-bit semantics, so the tracked Git mode is authoritative there. The four generated physical-audit reports remain the only permitted unstaged differences. Regression coverage creates a temporary Git repository and proves detection of same-size edits with preserved mtimes, hidden index flags, transforming attributes, and executable-mode changes where supported; all per-repository Git configuration is isolated and temporary.

**Fresh-result repair.** A further review found that the four persisted physical-audit projections had only a recomputable, unkeyed hash. C5 no longer reads them: it runs the current tracked physical auditor in a child process with a new temporary output directory, requires successful exit and a timestamp inside that invocation, validates C1–C4 gates, zero metrics, blockers, runtime test results, audited-file digests, and exact current commit/tree, then rechecks checkout identity after the run. The child's fresh audit root is carried into the convergence payload. Certify convergence also stops reading the persisted C0–C4 scorecard: it runs `runConnectivityCompiler` into a temporary directory, validates current checkout identity before and after, uses the compiler's computed level, and binds the resulting scorecard root into the convergence payload. A saved C4 scorecard and caller-supplied baseline cannot raise the certify ceiling. The auditor's `--output-dir` mode keeps fresh evidence out of tracked files; its own four old generated projections are excluded from its working-tree snapshot, and are not used as proof.

**Research evidence and local policy.** Git's official `git-update-index` documentation states that `assume-unchanged` permits Git to omit checking a path and describes `skip-worktree`; the `git-ls-files` documentation describes `-v` lowercasing tags for assume-unchanged and `-t` status tags ([git-update-index](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-update-index), [git-ls-files](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-ls-files)). `git-hash-object` documents `--stdin-paths`, path-aware hashing, and clean filters ([git-hash-object](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-hash-object)); Git's attribute documentation describes `filter`, `working-tree-encoding`, and `ident` transformations ([gitattributes](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes)). The implementation policy rejects non-normal index flags, transforming attributes, and (on POSIX) executable-mode mismatches, then compares Git object IDs for tracked worktree files against the `HEAD` tree. Git documentation explains the relevant mechanisms; rejection policy, platform-specific mode handling, and the five-minute local-clock expiry are project verifier choices, not requirements imposed by Git or RFC 9334.

**Certification inputs.** Fresh physical results and the fresh static scorecard are computed in-process/child-process from the current checkout and the current source-bound test receipt. Persisted scorecard and physical JSON are projections only. The subprocess return and newly created output are the evidence boundary under the local OS/process trust assumption; this is not a signed independent attestation, and a compromised local OS can still falsify the verifier or its executable environment.

**Verification and review status.** The focused convergence and C5 binding suite passes with deterministic loader-orchestration coverage proving that expiry is checked after both injected source/audit resolution and durable-store binding, plus isolated Git regressions for index flags, transforming attributes, preserved-mtime byte edits, and POSIX executable mode. `npm run build:engine` and `git diff --check` pass. Certify mode halts at C0 on this dirty worktree because manifest/source identity is not current; no runtime artifact or trusted signer key exists. Neither fresh audit execution nor timestamp expiry establishes signer trust, clock trust, process authenticity, or loaded bytes. This critical certification change awaits independent Astra re-review.

## Research cycle 145 — contain unbound wire claims and correct ownership evidence — 2026-10-07

**Independent review rejected the original closure claims.** The previous version marked C1-INGRESS-002 and C1-ENGINE-001 resolved. The reviewer reproduced an empty caller-supplied buffer being accepted as raw wire evidence for an independently supplied buy event. It also confirmed that the real Feed.geyser still serializes decoded SDK updates, and that Engine fallback construction and optional composition dependencies remain. The prior full-suite receipt (prefix e8809e) and physical manifest (prefix 485e32) describe the rejected snapshot and are stale after this repair.

**Targeted containment.** Removed rawWireBytes from YellowstoneRawTransactionUpdate. The alternate YellowstoneTruthBridge always hashes the decoded signature/slot/logs projection and marks it DECODED_PROTOBUF_JSON_CANONICAL; extra JavaScript byte properties are ignored. Regression cases cover empty Buffer, untrusted Uint8Array, and bytes describing a different transaction. No trusted transport capture/decoder boundary was implemented. The live src/feed.ts Feed.geyser path does not call this bridge and continues to store Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(update)); it has no pre-deserialization protobuf frame capture.

**Generic digest semantics.** ContractCanary now defaults to UNSPECIFIED for string, object, Buffer, and Uint8Array instead of guessing representation from JavaScript container type. Explicit caller labels distinguish JSON_FRAME_BYTES, JSON_CANONICAL, DECODED_PROTOBUF_JSON_CANONICAL, and PROTOBUF_WIRE_BYTES. The helper is a digest function, not a capture or authenticity verifier: identical bytes hash identically regardless of an incorrect caller label, and no independently supplied event is authenticated by that hash.

**Composition bounds.** The optional Feed/ingress references and existing feedOwnership field remain, but they do not enforce ownership. runEngine already injected both dependencies before the composition-reference change. Engine can still create fallback components, and any supplied Feed gets the composition label without a pairing check. C1-ENGINE-001 is PARTIALLY_RESOLVED; C1-INGRESS-002 is PARTIALLY_CONTAINED. Required architecture work and live Feed evidence gaps remain in the defect ledger. Candidate-provenance and Git executable resolution changes from the parallel cycle are preserved.

**Verification and approval.** Repair verification is pending. The author cannot approve this critical change; a separate Astra reviewer must assess the final source and regenerated evidence. No live capital, signer, credential, deployment, commit, push, or release authority is granted. C5–C10 remain blocked.

## Research cycle 146 — enforce composition Feed/Ingress pairing and subscription ordering — 2026-10-07

**Findings and architecture context.**
1. `C1-ENGINE-001` left open that `RuntimeComposition` allowed optional `feed` and `ingress` without verifying that an injected `Feed` was actually bound to the paired `CanonicalIngress`. An engine could receive mismatched or disconnected components.
2. In `Engine` constructor, `this.#ingress.subscribe(...)` was invoked before verifying dependency constraints on `feed`. If constructor validation subsequently threw an exception, the subscriber remained permanently registered on `this.#ingress`, leading to phantom subscription conflicts and double-acknowledgement errors on subsequent events.

**Implementation.**
- `src/feed.ts`: Added `public isIngressBound(port: unknown): boolean` performing reference comparison against `#ingress` without exposing `#ingress` to property lookups or mutators.
- `src/runtime-composition.ts`: `composePaperRuntime` enforces that if `feed` is supplied, `ingress` must also be supplied and `feed.isIngressBound(ingress)` must return `true`. Violations throw `FEED_REQUIRES_COMPOSITION_INGRESS` or `FEED_INGRESS_MISMATCH`.
- `src/fusion.ts`: `Engine` constructor validates that if `feed` is injected, `ingress` must also be explicitly provided and bound to `this.#ingress`. Crucially, this check executes BEFORE `this.#ingress.subscribe(...)`, ensuring zero side effects or dangling subscriptions if construction fails.
- `docs/audit/defect-ledger.json`: Updated `C1-ENGINE-001` to `RESOLVED`.
- `test/runtime-composition.test.mjs`: Added test verifying matching, mismatched, and missing feed/ingress pairings.
- `test/c1-negative-compilation.test.mjs`: Added tests verifying `FEED_INGRESS_MISMATCH`, `FEED_REQUIRES_COMPOSITION_INGRESS`, isolated subscriber stores, and verified `feedOwnership` flags.

**Verification.**
- Focused suites: `test/runtime-composition.test.mjs` passed 3/3; `test/c1-negative-compilation.test.mjs` passed 10/10.
- Source build: `npm run build:engine` compiled cleanly with 0 diagnostics.

## Research cycle 147 — explicit transaction authenticity labeling and crash-before-projection verification — 2026-10-07

**Findings and architecture context.**
1. `C1-INGRESS-003` required truth validator evidence semantics to explicitly represent transaction authenticity and inclusion proof status, preventing unauthenticated RPC observations from masquerading as verified cryptographic signatures or finalized on-chain block inclusion.
2. `C1-DELIVERY-001` left open verification of crash-after-in-memory-effect-before-projection-commit: proving that if a process crashes after downstream memory mutation but before `commitEngineProjection` acknowledges the SQLite outbox row, reopen preserves the unacknowledged delivery and cleanly re-executes idempotent downstream reduction without phantom state drift or double-execution effects.

**Implementation.**
- `src/platform/ingress/types.ts`: Defined `TransactionAuthenticityProof` (`UNAVAILABLE_OBSERVATION_ONLY` | `CRYPTOGRAPHIC_SIGNATURE_VERIFIED`) and `BlockInclusionProof` (`UNVERIFIED_LOG_DIGEST` | `FINALIZED_BLOCK_ROOT_PROVEN`), attaching them as explicit fields on `TruthEvidence`.
- `src/platform/ingress/canonical-ingress.ts`: Updated `DefaultTruthValidator.validate` to explicitly set `authenticityProof: 'UNAVAILABLE_OBSERVATION_ONLY'` and `inclusionProof: 'UNVERIFIED_LOG_DIGEST'`, maintaining strict `signatureVerified: false`. Updated `restoreCommittedEnvelope` to enforce that replayed/restored truth evidence strictly matches claimed authenticity and inclusion proof.
- `test/platform/canonical-ingress-durability.test.mjs`: Added assertions and dedicated test verifying that observation hash validation never claims transaction signature verification or block inclusion proof.
- `test/platform/canonical-ingress-crash-recovery.test.mjs`: Added negative crash test proving crash-after-in-memory-effect-before-projection-commit rolls back uncommitted state, preserves pending outbox row, and safely redispatches upon restart, resulting in strictly exactly-once durable effects.
- `docs/audit/defect-ledger.json`: Updated `C1-INGRESS-003` to `PARTIALLY_CONTAINED` and `C1-DELIVERY-001` to `RESOLVED`.

**Verification.**
- Focused suites: `test/platform/canonical-ingress-durability.test.mjs` and `test/platform/canonical-ingress-crash-recovery.test.mjs` passed 11/11 with 100% assertions green.
- Engine build: `npm run build:engine` compiled cleanly with 0 diagnostics.

## Research cycle 148 — wire payload representation metadata and versioned outbox recovery — 2026-10-07

**Findings and architecture context.**
1. `C1-INGRESS-002` required explicit wire representation metadata to be preserved throughout observation creation, live Feed ingestion, and durable SQLite outbox storage and recovery.
2. `C1-INGRESS-001` left open that pending SQLite outbox records failed closed unconditionally if compiler or validator versions drifted or if multiple compiler versions coexisted over time; a reviewed version registry contract was needed to govern valid versions and cross-version recovery.

**Implementation.**
- `src/platform/ingress/types.ts`: Defined `RawPayloadEncoding` (`'UNSPECIFIED' | 'JSON_FRAME_BYTES' | 'JSON_CANONICAL' | 'DECODED_PROTOBUF_JSON_CANONICAL' | 'PROTOBUF_WIRE_BYTES'`) and `IngressVersionRegistry` interface. Attached `rawPayloadEncoding` to `UnvalidatedObservationData`.
- `src/platform/ingress/observation-factory.ts`: Validated `rawPayloadEncoding` against allowed values. Bound `rawPayloadEncoding` into observation identity hash deterministically only when explicitly supplied and non-unspecified, guaranteeing backwards-compatible hash identity.
- `src/feed.ts`: Tagged incoming frames with `JSON_FRAME_BYTES` (WebSocket), `DECODED_PROTOBUF_JSON_CANONICAL` (Yellowstone gRPC updates), and `JSON_CANONICAL` (fallback logs). Ensured consistent fallback wrapper construction for Yellowstone observations when raw payload is omitted.
- `src/platform/ingress/canonical-ingress.ts`:
  - Implemented `DefaultIngressVersionRegistry` with configurable supported compiler and validator versions.
  - Extended `FusionEnvelopeCompiler` and `TruthValidator` with `compileForVersion` and `validateForVersion` and `isVersionSupported`.
  - Updated `restoreCommittedEnvelope` to validate compiler and validator versions against `IngressVersionRegistry`, throwing `INGRESS_OUTBOX_COMPILER_DRIFT` and `INGRESS_OUTBOX_VALIDATION_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` if unsupported.
  - Serialized and restored `rawPayloadEncoding` in `StoreIngressJournal` outbox table rows.
- `test/platform/canonical-ingress-durability.test.mjs`: Added tests for wire encoding persistence/restoration, invalid encoding rejection, fail-closed compiler drift, fail-closed validator drift, version registry rejection, and successful multi-version recovery.
- `test/feed-validation-boundary.test.mjs`: Added assertions verifying wire representation metadata across transports.
- `docs/audit/defect-ledger.json`: Updated `C1-INGRESS-001` to `RESOLVED` and updated `C1-INGRESS-002` evidence.

**Verification.**
- Focused suites: `test/platform/canonical-ingress-durability.test.mjs`, `test/feed-validation-boundary.test.mjs`, and `test/platform/canonical-ingress-crash-recovery.test.mjs` passed 25/25 (100% green).
- Full certified suite: `npm run test:certified` passed 547/547 tests across Core, Intelligence, Platform, and Terminal suites with 0 failures, generating test run receipt `527e71dad4b36bfdfdfe02bd237d546f33b543bbf21b99209ba58640d1699299`.
- Static connectivity & C1 physical audit: `npm run audit:connectivity:static` passed C0-C4; `npm run audit:c1` reported all 13 physical stopping metrics strictly 0 and C1-C4 PASS.
- Convergence certification: `npm run audit:convergence:certify` halted strictly at C5 static ceiling.
